| `google_lead_form_hooks` | A lead source's Google Ads lead form webhook key (R-GADS-1) | lead_source_id, key_hash (sha256, the only routing input; global UQ), key_enc (envelope-encrypted key, re-showable to Owner/Admin), inquiry_type (buy/sell/rent/value/general), cites_source_disclosure, created_by_user_id, retired_at, last_test_at, last_unreadable_at, unreadable_count | one current hook per source (partial UQ); identity, key_hash and key_enc immutable; retirement is final and a retired row takes no further writes (trigger); never deleted (a retired hash is never re-issued) | read: Owner/Admin/system (support never); write: system only (the API re-reads the role under the system actor) | key_enc **secret**; key_hash plain; counters/times plain |
| `lead_source_webhook_secrets` | A lead source's own X-Realty-Signature secret (R-LSRC-3) | PK (tenant_id, lead_source_id), secret_enc, created_by_user_id, created_at | rotation replaces the secret in place; the row's identity never moves; no DELETE | system only (read and write); not a column of `lead_sources`, which every member reads | secret_enc **secret** |
| `lead_source_skips` | One receipt per partner-feed lead skipped because its source was off (R-LSRC-2, E597) | lead_source_id, channel (`partner_feed`), external_ref_sha256 (sha256 of the feed's own lead id; UQ per source and channel), reason (`SOURCE_OFF`), skipped_at | append-only (no UPDATE or DELETE); no lead content | read: Owner/Admin/system (support read-only); write: system only (the feed poll) | plain (a hash, codes, times) |

Changed: `phone_numbers.lead_source_id` (composite FK to `lead_sources`; CHECK `lead_source_id IS NULL OR purpose = 'tracking'`; partial index on live tied numbers; the Layer 2a policies apply unchanged; R-TRK-1). `lead_sources.active_changed_at` (written only by off/on; the card's "Off since"). `webhook_events.last_attempt_at` (NULL means `received_at`; R-LSRC-7). `lead_email_messages`: `hold_code` adds `PERSONAL_MAILBOX` and `FORWARDING_CONFIRMATION`, `parser_kind` adds `leadmetadata` (named CHECKs replaced). Index `lead_inquiries (tenant_id, lead_source_id, received_at DESC)` for the overview's last lead and 30-day count.

Definers (SECURITY REVIEW ITEMS, ARCHITECTURE §21): `app.resolve_google_lead_form(bytea)` → (tenant_id, hook_id, lead_source_id), pre-tenant, STABLE, 32-byte input, only the current hook of an active source in an active tenant, writes nothing; `app.rearm_webhook_event(provider, provider_event_id, now)` → uuid, one conditional UPDATE that re-arms a signed, routed delivery whose run failed, or stayed `received` past the 5-minute stale window (**VERIFY 15**), never a processed, unrouted or rejected one. Both owned by `app_definer`, pinned `search_path`, EXECUTE for `app_user` only.

A Google lead form is **not** an `integration_connections` kind: it has no external account and no OAuth, only the system writes it, and a new kind would widen 0018's live-account CHECK and the guard's exemption list. The integrations page's `ads_google` (module 52's OAuth account) stays separate.

### 6.4 Photo and video texts (owner decision D5; migration `0055_outbound_mms.sql`, renumbered at merge)

| Table | Purpose | Key fields | Invariants | Access (RLS) | Field classes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `outbound_media_uploads` | A sender's staged photo or video for a one-to-one text, before it is attached to a message | (tenant_id, id); contact_id (→ contacts, ON DELETE CASCADE); uploaded_by_user_id (→ memberships); object_id (→ stored_objects, UNIQUE per tenant); content_type (JPEG, PNG, GIF, MP4, QuickTime); byte_size (> 0, ≤ 500,000 for video, ≤ 5,000,000 otherwise); created_at, expires_at | created_at and expires_at (created + 24 h) are set by the DB (`app.outbound_media_uploads_stamp`); the row names an unshredded `mms` object the uploader created, with the same type and size; deleting it shreds an unattached object (`app.outbound_media_upload_shred`); no UPDATE for any role | Read and delete by the uploader or the system; insert by the uploader for a person they see in detail; staff Layer 1 | content_type, byte_size: internal; the object: encrypted (per-object DEK, purpose `mms`) |

`messages`: CHECK `messages_mms_one_to_one_human` (an outbound, not imported `mms` is a human send with no bulk action, assistant action, playbook step run or marketing piece); trigger `messages_mms_channel_fixed` (no message moves into or out of `mms`); deferred constraint trigger `messages_mms_has_media` (an outbound `mms` has at least one `message_media` row at commit). `message_media`: the sender's insert policy `media_outbound_sender_insert` and the guard trigger `app.message_media_outbound_guard` (every inserter: an outbound parent must be the caller's own queued or scheduled human `mms`; positions 0–9, no provider ref; the object an unexpired upload of that sender for that person; at most 10 items and 5,000,000 bytes with the text). Attached media never change (no UPDATE policy, no DELETE grant).

### 6.y Texting reactions (D5b, 2026-11-11; migration `*_sms_reactions.sql`, renumbered at merge)
| `message_reactions` | Links an inbound reaction text to the team's outbound text it quotes | message_id (the reaction text; UQ), target_message_id, action (`add`/`remove`), reaction (`love`/`like`/`dislike`/`laugh`/`emphasize`/`question`/`sticker`/`emoji`), emoji (as received, 1–16 code points, only for `emoji`), whole_text_reading (the classifier's reading of the whole text, kept for review) | written once by the system in the inbound transaction; never updated; deleted only with either message (both FKs cascade); `app.message_reactions_guard` (invoker): system only, and only a non-imported inbound SMS linked to a non-imported outbound `sms`/`mms` text on a record of the same person (`app.contact_person_ids`: the root survivor and every record merged into it, so the team's text still counts while a Team Lead's merge has not yet moved it; migration `b8_review_texting`), sent from the number the reaction came in on to its sender, at least `dispatching` and not failed, dispatched before it and created in the 30 days before (R-REACT-2, R-REACT-5) | read: whoever reads the reaction message; insert: system only; no UPDATE/DELETE grant | emoji, reaction, whole_text_reading PII (fail-closed column rule; never in an audit diff); ids and action plain |
## 7. Phase 2-3 RLS and the client principal

- **Two principals.** `app.principal_type()` is `staff` (Phase 1) or `client` (Phase 3 portal). A client context sets `app.tenant_id`, `app.principal_type = 'client'` and `app.portal_account_id` from the portal session resolver only (module 43); `app.user_id` and `app.actor_kind` stay empty. `app.portal_account_id()` returns NULL unless the principal is `client`, so a staff session that sets the GUC gains nothing (smoke). `my_role()`, `is_member()` and `is_tenant_wide()` are false for a client.
- **Layer 1.** Every table that existed before 0015, and all 15 new staff tables, keep `tenant_id = app.tenant_id() AND principal_type = 'staff'`. Exactly three tables (`portal_accounts`, `portal_grants`, `portal_items`) use `principal_type IN ('staff','client')` for reads, keep staff-only `WITH CHECK`, add RESTRICTIVE `client_read_only_{ins,upd,del}`, and their triggers refuse a client principal outright (`CLIENT_READ_ONLY`). 0015's self-check fails the migration if any other table admits a client or loses its staff-only Layer 1.
- **Client Layer 2.** Own active account → own unrevoked, unexpired grants of that account on a deal or listing its contact is still a party to (`app.portal_party_ok`, a boolean definer; removing a co-buyer hides everything at once) → unretracted items of those grants (each subquery runs under the client's own policies). Expiry compares with `now()`, so a lapse is immediate. A client sees no staff table, no view and no other tenant (generated sweep in the smoke file).
- **Binding.** A grant can only name a party's own deal or listing and never moves; an item copies only a source of its grant's own deal or listing and never moves; revocation and retraction are final (all actors, the tenant-wide sync job included). `portal.document_object` (module 43, `0023_p3_portal_auth.sql`) re-checks the item, its grant, the document's scope, `shared_to_portal` and `scan_status` at download.
- **Staff policies on portal tables** all require `principal_type = 'staff'` explicitly.
- **Not principals:** web-chat visitors and public page viewers. They never get a tenant GUC; their reads go through definers the owning modules add (`app.webchat_poll`, `app.public_listing_page`), each a security-review item.
- **Definers added by 0015:** `app.ad_allocation_cap_check()` (deferred constraint trigger), `app.contacts_dnc_follow_merge()` (trigger), `app.portal_party_ok(uuid,uuid,uuid,uuid)` (boolean); all app_definer, pinned search_path. The two dispatch guards are replaced (same owner and search_path), and so are `app.purge_contact_internal` and `app.retention_sweep` (app_retention). `app_definer` gets column-level UPDATE on `contacts (do_not_contact_at, do_not_contact_event_id)` only. Planned later (each in its module's migration, each a review item): `app.resolve_channel_account`, `app.resolve_webchat_widget`, `app.webchat_session`, `app.webchat_poll`, `app.public_listing_page`, `app.record_person_opt_out` / `app.reinstate_person_contact` / `app.undo_language_opt_out`, `portal.request_login`, `portal.consume_login`, `portal.resolve_session`, `portal.document_object`, `app.coaching_trends`.
- **Definers added by the D2 migration** (both app_definer, pinned search_path, EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC, each a security-review item): `app.mail_copy_live(tenant, copy)` (boolean; takes only the copy's own row identity and looks the row up itself, S-D2-4: the context tenant, the copy's link live, its thread not excluded, its contact point current and confirmed in `mail_sync_points`, its address no live link's mailbox; granted to `app_user` for the RESTRICTIVE read policy only; since the D2 review fixes it answers only a caller the `mail_messages` read policies admit for that row: a staff principal, a user or system actor, and the system, the link's own user or a reader of the contact's timeline, tested through its own copy of `app.contact_detail_visible` because a definer reads `contacts` without the caller's RLS; false for a support actor, a client principal, no actor and a peer who holds the copy's id) and the trigger function `app.mail_sync_points_guard()` (no grant; it checks a confirmation against every live link's mailbox HMAC and a deal-only contact's points, which the confirming user cannot read, and returns at once unless the context tenant is set and is the row's). The draft's `app.mail_sync_live(tenant, link, thread)` is not created: it answered for any link and thread by id, an oracle for any member (S-D2-4). `app.mail_link_live(tenant, link)` and `app.mail_contact_related(tenant, user, contact)` are invoker functions (they reveal nothing the caller's own RLS does not show); the `mail_messages` insert guard is an invoker trigger, because only the system actor passes it and the system sees every link, exclusion and confirmation.
- **D2 review fixes** (one migration after the D2 one; post-merge review of the D2 schema): it replaces two definers with the same owner, search_path, volatility and grants, `app.resolve_channel_account` (0024's body without `ads_meta`: D2 has no Meta ads, so no Meta kind routes) and `app.mail_copy_live` (above); adds the invoker trigger `app.messages_rfc_guard` (R-GMAIL-12); adds the source `wrong_number` to `consent_events_synced_gmail` (R-GMAIL-9); reorders `mail_messages_select` (row-identical); and appends the rollout precondition to the `platform:gmail` notes. Proof: `packages/db/test/rls_smoke_gmail_review.sql`.
- **Gmail visibility model (D2).** `mail_messages` is read exactly like the contact's Phase 1 timeline (`app.contact_detail_visible`) plus the mailbox owner for their own link's rows; RESTRICTIVE `mail_messages_live`: only while `app.mail_copy_live` holds, so a disconnect, a deactivated member, the switch off, the pack uncleared, an exclusion, a removed contact point or a withdrawn confirmation hides the copies at once (the system still sees them, to delete them; S-D2-2); RESTRICTIVE `mail_messages_no_support`: never a support actor, even under an Owner support grant (**VERIFY** Google API Services User Data Policy); Layer 1 staff only, so never a client principal. `user_mail_links`, `mail_thread_exclusions` and `mail_sync_points` are self and system only. Team visibility stays the timeline audience (the task requirement; owner question Q-GMAIL-5, S-D2-8 a), and the connect disclosure says so. No database barrier keeps synced mail from an AI model (one login role; a GUC the caller sets is no barrier against the code it guards, S-D2-8 b): the enforcement is module 68's architecture test (no SQL names `mail_messages` outside `mailSync/**`; `mailSync/**` imports no model provider).
- **Photo and video texts (D5).**
  - `outbound_media_uploads` keeps the staff Layer 1. It is read and deleted by the uploader or the system, and inserted only by the uploader for a person they see in detail. There is no UPDATE.
  - `media_outbound_sender_insert` lets a message's sender attach to their own queued or scheduled `mms`. `app.message_media_outbound_guard` re-checks every insert, a tenant-wide actor's included.
  - The definer `app.outbound_media_upload_shred` (owner app_retention, pinned search_path, no PUBLIC EXECUTE) shreds only the deleted upload's own unattached `mms` object.
- **Superuser sessions** (the migration owner, test fixtures) are maintenance: `assistant_actions_decision_guard` does not bind them; no application path runs as a superuser (ARCHITECTURE §16). Every other 0015 guard also runs when no tenant context is set (`app.tenant_id() IS NOT NULL AND …` early return, as Phase 1's), so a definer or pre-tenant path cannot skip it.

TCs do not publish to the portal: confirmed milestones are rule-published by `portal.sync` (system), and a TC who needs another item asks the deal's agent (aligned with RLS: publishing needs deal write access).

## 9. Phase 2-3 business rules

Notation as §4. **smoke** here means `rls_smoke_p23.sql`.

### Tenancy, integrations, channels
- **R-TEN-11** Every new webhook resolves its tenant only through an enumerated definer that returns `(tenant_id, connection_id)` for a **connected or degraded** connection (a `pending_review` row reserves the account but routes nothing); payload fields never choose the tenant; inbound for an account with no such connection is `unrouted` with a platform alert. [DB definer + api]
- **R-TEN-12** Web-chat visitors and public-page viewers are not principals: no tenant GUC, reads only through `app.webchat_poll` (that session's outbound messages) and `app.public_listing_page` (one published snapshot), each returning minimal columns. [DB definer + api]
- **R-TEN-13** Every table keeps the staff-only Layer 1 except the three portal tables; the migration self-check enforces the allowlist. [DB self-check]
- **R-TEN-14** `app.portal_account_id()` is NULL unless `principal_type = 'client'`; the portal resolver is the only code that sets a client context, and staff and client transaction helpers are separate (`withTenantTx` / `withPortalTx`, lint). [DB + api + lint]
- **R-INT-1** Every external service is an adapter interface with a deterministic fake; tests and dev never reach a real service; a real adapter refuses to start without its configuration. Platform-procured vendors (AVM, public records, voice stack, malware scanner, text extractor) are platform configuration, not tenant connections. [api + architecture test]
- **R-INT-2** The UI offers only capabilities the adapter reports (`capabilities` is written only by the system), states the connection's status in words (e.g. "needs partner access"), and a connection that is not `connected`/`degraded` sends, publishes and polls nothing. [api + web + DB trigger]
- **R-INT-3** An external account (Page, IG account, MLS feed, ad account, GBP location, partner feed) is live in at most one tenant (global unique index over `pending_review/connected/degraded`). Only the provider callback (the system actor, after the adapter proved the token manages that account) links `external_account_id` or `credential_id` or moves an external kind into a live status; a live external kind needs both; the account id never changes (a different account is a new row). Owner/Admin create unconfigured rows, rename, set sources, widget origins and lead-email sender domains, edit the MLS licence pack and disconnect. Web-chat widgets and lead inboxes have no external account (their keys are server-generated) and may be switched on by Owner/Admin. [DB trigger, smoke]
- **R-INT-4** Adapters take explicit ids and credentials decrypted only by the system actor. [api]
- **R-INT-5** Disconnecting, or removing the member who owns a calendar link, revokes the grant at the provider (best effort) and locally in the same transaction; tokens are purged within 30 days (calendar links: by `calendar.reconcile`, hourly, which revokes the grant and then purges the token the 0049 trigger kept; `app.retention_sweep` does not cover `user_calendar_links`). **Suspension is not removal, for calendar and Gmail links alike** (b7 review): while the membership is `suspended` (or `deactivating`, or `invited`) the link is kept with its grant, it is hidden and its sync is paused (no push, no Gmail pass; Gmail copies are hidden by `app.mail_link_live`, never deleted); only `removed` revokes. On reactivation sync resumes by itself: Gmail at its next pass; for calendars, `calendar.reconcile` (hourly) enqueues every change the active member's connected calendar has not accepted yet (pushes that paused as `member_inactive`). A suspended member cannot sign in, so they never see their link while suspended; the link's status sentence is "Paused while your account is suspended." wherever it is rendered, and after reactivation the page shows it connected again, never revoked. The calendar page names a link in `pending_review` as its own waiting state ("Waiting for approval", never "Connected"), and while the deployment runs the fake adapters it says "This deployment uses a test calendar: nothing reaches your real calendar." and "Connected (test)" (batch 7 review ui/F3). [api + retention]
- **R-INT-6** A capability that waits on counsel review or a vendor check is gated on its published platform pack (`jurisdiction_packs` key `platform:<name>`) having `reviewed_by_counsel_at` set (and, for voice, `rules.vendor_ready_at`): Messenger/Instagram sending (`platform:meta`, DB guard), voice answering (`platform:voice`, DB at the toggle and at every AI call), publishing marketing (`platform:fair_housing`, DB), turning call scoring on (`platform:coaching`, DB), home-value notes (`platform:avm`, core), review requests (`platform:review_requests`, core), portal sign-in (`platform:portal`, module 43's definer), Gmail inbox sync (`platform:gmail`, which also needs `rules.vendor_ready_at`; DB at the Owner switch, the link and every synced row). **D2:** Messenger/Instagram sending is out of scope and `platform:meta` is never cleared, so the DB guard branch always refuses. The packs ship unreviewed; the UI says so; a later unreviewed version turns the capability off again. Replaces "feature flags". [DB + core, smoke]
- **R-CHAN-1** A Messenger, Instagram or web-chat identity is a `contact_points` row whose HMAC covers `{platform}:{external_account_id}:{scoped_id}`; its opt-outs are address-keyed exactly like phones and emails (R-CONSENT-1 unchanged). D2: only web chat is built; the Messenger/Instagram address forms stay in the CHECK until the cleanup, which retires this rule's Messenger/Instagram half, and no product path writes them. [DB + core]
- **R-CHAN-2** A person-level do-not-contact (`contacts.do_not_contact_at`, citing a revoke in the consent ledger) makes both dispatch guards refuse every message and call to that contact on every channel, including points added later, for every initiator (only the Phase 1 STOP/HELP reply to the number that sent the keyword is exempt); nobody on the team can clear it. A merge never loses it: the survivor takes the earliest one (definer trigger, along merge chains), and the guards also refuse a recipient a do-not-contact duplicate was merged into. A merged-away record is the whole person (F1, `0043_merged_person_opt_outs` `app.contact_person_ids`): canContact and both guards judge a recipient by every record of its person (the root survivor reached through `merged_into_contact_id` and every record merged into it), so the survivor's do-not-contact, and an opt-out on a phone only the survivor had, block a send addressed to the merged-away record exactly as one addressed to the survivor; and nothing new is addressed to a merged-away record (a text, email, call or enrollment of it is refused 409 `CONTACT_MERGED`, its saved searches send no alert). It is said before the tap wherever a send, call or chat reply is offered (batch 6 review): the reachability summary carries `PERSON_DO_NOT_CONTACT` on every channel and point, the website chat included, also a point with no revoke of its own (a survivor's own phone, a point added later, the chat after "stop texting me"), in its own words ("Asked not to be contacted at all"), and the feed's channel hint denies every channel, so no Text, Email, Call or chat Reply is offered and nothing invites adding what the person typed in a chat. [DB, smoke]
- **R-CHAN-3** Opt-out language on any channel ("stop contacting me"), and an agent-recorded "do not contact me", set the person-level do-not-contact and revoke every channel on every point of the contact (`needs_review` for language). From a web-chat session this is always the session's own contact (a chat session is never attached to an existing contact by what the visitor typed, R-INBOX-1), so an anonymous visitor can never silence someone else. "Not an opt-out" (audited, reason required) reverses only what that language event wrote, through the reviewed definer. Closing a lead with reason `do_not_contact` records the same person-level opt-out in the same transaction (module 22). This tightens R-CONSENT-8 and never loosens it. [core + db]
- **R-CHAN-4** An exact keyword (STOP …) on Messenger or Instagram revokes that channel on every point of that kind on the contact and cancels queued messages on it; at most one confirmation goes to the sending identity within 24 h. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL** (keyword list per channel). D2: no Messenger/Instagram inbound exists, so no product path receives such a keyword; the 0018 DB guard branch and its smoke check stay until the cleanup, which retires this rule. [core + DB guard, smoke]
- **R-CHAN-5** `core-compliance/3.0.0` `canContact` blocks at least what the DB guard blocks and adds `PERSON_DO_NOT_CONTACT`, `CHANNEL_NOT_CONNECTED` and `PLATFORM_NOT_CLEARED` (operational, not consent: D1 stands); `messenger` and `instagram` are always denied `PLATFORM_NOT_CLEARED`; the determinism and monotonic-denial property tests cover every new input; one shared case table (`packages/db/test/fixtures/opt_out_cases.json`) drives both the DB guard test and the core gate test. D2 history: the platform-window input and `PLATFORM_WINDOW_CLOSED` were dropped with Meta DMs. [core + db test]
- **R-GATE-13** `messages_dispatch_guard` maps every channel explicitly and raises `CHANNEL_UNKNOWN` for anything else (no `ELSE 'sms'` fallthrough); adding a channel changes the `messages.channel` CHECK, the guard map and core's `Channel` union together (architecture test: the three lists are equal). D2: core's `Channel` union keeps `messenger`/`instagram` only to equal the 0018 CHECK; both are always denied. [DB + architecture test]
- **R-GATE-14** Every new outbound path (DM and chat replies (D2: web chat only), open-house invites, seller updates, milestone updates, home-value notes, review requests, portal login links and notices, voice booking confirmations) goes through `dispatchOutbound` and has a path test showing a blocked send writes `gate_decisions` and reaches no provider. [api + DB]
- **R-GATE-15** The reserved outbound method names `deliverChannelMessage` (`comms/dispatch.ts`, web chat), `publishPost` (Facebook Pages, Instagram business accounts and GBP locations, R-MKT-12), `postReviewReply` (`marketing/publish.ts`) and `applyBudgetChange` (`ads/dispatch.ts`) are callable only from their single path module; no interface has a method that places an AI outbound call. D2 history: `sendDirectMessage` was dropped with Meta DMs. [lint + architecture test]

### Unified inbox
- **R-INBOX-1** Every channel's messages live in the contact's one conversation; an identity with no exact match creates a contact through intake routing with the connection's lead source, never by name match (a name match only suggests a duplicate; merge is R-OWN-24). A phone or email typed into web chat is an unverified claim (`webchat_sessions.claimed_*`): it never attaches the session to an existing contact and never becomes a contact point by itself; a match only raises a duplicate suggestion that says "typed in chat, not verified". A merge (a human decision, R-OWN-24) moves the person's chat sessions, and so their claims, to the survivor: the survivor answers the open chat, and a returning visitor's claim matches the survivor. [core + db]
- **R-INBOX-2** Inbound on every new channel runs the Phase 1 ingestion transaction: pause every enrollment on every contact sharing the identity, cancel queued assistant messages **and scheduled trigger runs of those contacts** (module 22 cancels them by contact; module 26's `executeTriggerRun` re-checks the pause), create exactly one handback. [db/api tests per channel]
- **R-INBOX-3** D2: the DM part is out of scope (the 0018 guard branch stays until the cleanup, which retires this rule, and always refuses `PLATFORM_NOT_CLEARED`); web chat is unchanged. The dispatch guard maps Messenger/Instagram/web chat to their own consent channel and point kind; an outbound DM must name its connection, and a DM to an identity that opted out (on that identity or any of the person's identities of that kind: every record of the person since 0043, and canContact reads the same set since the batch 6 review), is suppressed, or belongs to a person-level do-not-contact is refused for every initiator. [DB, smoke]
- **R-INBOX-4** D2: DMs are out of scope, so `inbound_channel` resolves to web chat only. The assistant uses DM and chat channels only for the 60-second first response and human-approved drafts. The first response's step channel is `inbound_channel` ("reply where the lead wrote"), part of the hash-bound content the Owner approved; `PlaybookStep.channel` is `sms | email | inbound_channel`; no multi-turn autonomous replies. A chat draft (an `inbound_channel` step held for approval) is shown and judged as a chat reply everywhere (batch 6 review): the feed says "Chat reply drafted for you" and offers Reply in the chat or Send, judged by the chat and the person (never by the texts' opt-outs), and the approvals inbox captions it "Chat reply the assistant drafted". A handback after a chat reply offers Reply in the chat instead of "Can't reach them right now"; its suggested reply is the assistant's text or email (the server binds it to sms or email, sms even when there is no number), so its one-tap Send is judged by that draft's own channel, never by the chat: no number on file, or a STOP on it, and the card offers no Send (Reply in the chat first, the draft kept to edit), and the web disables a Send of a text or email to a lead with no number or address, with the reason (review chat-R2 of batch 6). A chat the gate summary blocks (the person's do-not-contact, or an opt-out of the chat, worded from its source: "Marked as a wrong number", "Asked in the chat to stop being contacted") offers no Reply there, and the card says that reason: first in "Can't reach them right now" when nothing else works, else as "Can't reply in the chat: …" (CHAT_BLOCKED, unscored; review chat-R3 of batch 6). [core]
- **R-INBOX-5** A web-chat session creates no contact, offer or notification until a first message that passes the spam checks and carries a name plus a phone or email, after the visitor saw the disclosure; the visitor reads only that session's outbound messages; nothing automated is ever sent to the typed phone or email (an agent may add it to the contact by hand and call or text, a human decision shown as not verified); per-widget and per-IP rate limits (`auth.rate_limit_hit`) cap sessions and messages; sessions with no contact are purged after 30 days. The embed code an Admin copies carries only `data-widget-key`; the widget fetches the team name and the current disclosure from the server, so a changed notice never goes stale on a customer's website (batch 7 review ui/F4). [api + DB definer + retention]
- **R-INBOX-6** **Retired: owner decision D2 (2026-11-11) put Messenger/Instagram DMs out of scope; no platform-window input, `PLATFORM_WINDOW_CLOSED` or window copy is built, and `platform_window_until` stays unused until the cleanup.** Old text: Platform messaging windows are data in the platform pack (`platform:meta`); the instant is stored on the point at inbound (`platform_window_until`); outside it every initiator is denied `PLATFORM_WINDOW_CLOSED` and compose offers the contact's other allowed channels. **VERIFY** current Meta policy (window length, human-agent tag). [core + api]
- **R-INBOX-7** Disconnecting a channel stops its sends at once (`CHANNEL_NOT_CONNECTED`); history, identities and opt-outs stay; reconnecting the same account in the same tenant restores identities by HMAC. [api]
- **R-INBOX-8** **Retired: owner decision D2 (2026-11-11): the product receives no Meta webhooks (no DMs, no Lead Ads; the publishing connection subscribes to none).** Old text: Meta webhook signatures are verified over the raw body before parsing; a bad signature writes only the rejected webhook row. [api]
- **R-INBOX-9** Portal and ad leads (Zillow, realtor.com, Meta Lead Ads) enter only through lead intake (routing, attribution, dedup). D2: Meta Lead Ads intake is removed; Zillow and Realtor.com stay, through partner feeds where access exists (`zillow_feed`, `realtor_feed`, `needs_partner_access` otherwise) and DKIM-aligned lead-email parsing. A lead email counts as a source lead only with a DKIM pass aligned to one of the connection's `allowed_sender_domains`; anything else is `needs_review` with no autonomous first response. A parsed lead email shows each extracted field with its source span; a field not found stays empty; no phone and no email ⇒ an Admin review card and no routing. [core + api]
- **R-LEM-1** Each lead source has at most one lead-email inbox (a keyed `lead_email` connection). Its address `leads+<key>@<inbound domain>` carries a server-generated 160-bit key; only sha256(key) routes; the key is stored envelope-encrypted so Owner/Admin can see and copy the address again; only Owner/Admin see it, and turning it on is 🔒 and audited (`lead_email.inbox_enabled`). [DB + api]
- **R-LEM-2** The key alone resolves tenant, inbox and lead source, through `app.resolve_lead_email_key` (never `app.resolve_channel_account`): only the current key of a connected/degraded inbox of an active lead source in an active tenant. Rotation (🔒, audited `lead_email.key_rotated`) retires the old key in the same transaction; a retired, guessed, malformed or other-domain address is unrouted and writes no tenant row. [DB + api]
- **R-LEM-3** Trust: a lead email creates a lead automatically ONLY when the receiving provider reports a DKIM pass whose signing domain and the From domain both sit under one of the inbox's `allowed_sender_domains` (relaxed alignment; the allowlist entry is the organizational domain). DKIM-Signature or Authentication-Results headers written by the sender are never evidence (Postmark: only the topmost Authentication-Results with the configured authserv-id). Anything else is held (`DKIM_MISSING`, `DKIM_FAILED`, `DKIM_UNALIGNED`, `SENDER_NOT_ALLOWED`, `FROM_INVALID`). [api]
- **R-LEM-4** A trusted email with a parsed, valid phone or email goes through the existing intake in the same transaction: dedupe by address, an inquiry with the inbox's lead source (`parse_status 'parsed'`, spans), routing through the ownership engine, and the 60-second first response to the LEAD's own phone or email; the portal's From address never becomes a contact point; opted-out addresses are never auto-messaged (D1, decided at send time). [api]
- **R-LEM-5** Held email (also `UNKNOWN_FORMAT`, `PARTIAL_FORMAT`, `NO_CONTACT_POINT`) creates no contact, no routing, no job and sends nothing; its subject, text and parse are stored encrypted with the reason, with the tenant-HMACs of the addresses it names (`point_hmacs`, keys only). The only transitions are `needs_review → accepted | discarded`, once, by a person (DB trigger), and, by the retention role only, `needs_review → erased` (the person it names was erased, R-DATA-4) or `→ expired` (30 days after receipt, R-DATA-3): both keep nothing of the person but the message key (migration 0041). An erased or expired email is not listed, accepted or discarded (`NOT_FOUND`). [DB + api]
- **R-LEM-6** Owner/Admin/Team Lead see the Needs review list with the reason in words; accepting runs the same intake (audited `lead_email.review_accepted`, the reviewer may type a missing phone/email/name) WITHOUT an autonomous first response (R-INBOX-9); discarding is audited (`lead_email.review_discarded`); both purge the stored content; a second decision is `LEAD_EMAIL_NOT_PENDING`; accepting with no contact point is `LEAD_EMAIL_NO_CONTACT_POINT`. [api]
- **R-LEM-7** An allowlisted sender domain is a registrable domain: no wildcard, no scheme, no bare public suffix (`com`, `co.uk`, …); lookalikes (`evilzillow.com`, `zillow.com.evil.test`) never match `zillow.com`. Seed suggestions `zillow.com`, `realtor.com` are **VERIFY**; the Owner sees each labelled by the portal's name and marked "suggested", with one sentence that they are unconfirmed until a test lead from each portal becomes a lead automatically (arriving is not enough: a test lead held for review confirms nothing; never the VERIFY marker). Changes are 🔒 and audited. Consumer mailbox domains are never allowed (R-LEM-11, `LEAD_EMAIL_DOMAIN_INVALID` with reason `consumer_mailbox`). The suggestions are a per-portal catalog (R-LEM-9): Zillow and Realtor.com pre-ticked, the others ticked only when the Owner picks that portal. [api]
- **R-LEM-8** The inbound webhook verifies the provider's signature (generic: X-Realty-Signature; Postmark/SendGrid: Basic), caps the body (1 MB JSON, 5 MB multipart) before parsing, drops attachments unread, strips HTML in linear time, never fetches links or images, and is replay-safe: webhook_events per inbox + message id, then the per-tenant UNIQUE message key in the lead's transaction (holds after the webhook_events purge); a sender-chosen Message-ID can never pre-empt another inbox. Parsed values carry no CR/LF, controls or bidi overrides. [api]
- **R-LEM-9** More portals' lead emails are read through the generic labelled format and leadmetadata, until real samples exist. Sender domains are offered per portal (Zillow, Realtor.com pre-ticked; Redfin, Homes.com/Homesnap, Trulia and Opcity on request; all **VERIFY**). Their domains are portal domains, whose addresses are never a lead's. Vendor rows for those portals wait for real sample emails and counsel's answer on the US 10,783,530 family. An email nothing reads is held (E78), and the page asks for a sample. [api + web]
- **R-LEM-10** leadmetadata.org v1.0 is read when an email's raw HTML declares it. Only real `lead_*` meta elements are read (head or body), never text made by entity decoding, a text/plain part, or comments, script, style, textarea or title. They are read in linear time, capped (64 tags, 2,000 characters a value) and cleaned, then prepended to the normalized body as labelled lines, so every field cites a span (R-INBOX-9). A name given twice with different values holds the email (`PARTIAL_FORMAT`). `lead_source` never changes the source. Other versions are not read. Trust is unchanged. [api]
- **R-LEM-11** Consumer mailbox domains are never allowed sender domains. The list is fixed and **VERIFY**. Saved entries are ignored and shown as not allowed. Mail from such a domain is held `PERSONAL_MAILBOX`, and only the forwarded block is read (no marker: nothing pre-filled). The parser keeps a lead's own address under a consumer domain. It never keeps the email's own From address, a member's account email or alert phone (any member who joined: active, suspended, deactivating or removed; b9 review ownership-data/F3, so a suspended forwarder's own signature never joins two leads), or one of the tenant's live numbers. It drops the portal's addresses and a non-consumer sender's own addresses. [api]
- **R-LEM-12** Automatic forwarding into a source address is supported. The key is read from every recipient, envelope included. It is trusted only through the original signature as the receiving provider reports it (**VERIFY** that the signature survives forwarding). Gmail's forwarding confirmation (from google.com, with an aligned google.com DKIM pass and the confirmation subject; **VERIFY**) is held `FORWARDING_CONFIRMATION` and says in words who asked. It is never a lead and cannot be accepted (Discard only, `LEAD_EMAIL_NOT_A_LEAD`), and the source shows that one is waiting. [api + web]

### Lead intake: sources, forms, tracking numbers

- **R-LSRC-1** Owner/Admin set up lead sources on one page. Each source shows its kind and a status in words, from stored facts only: last lead received, leads in the last 30 days, held emails waiting, off since when, and per path On / Off / Not set up / Not available on this deployment / Needs partner access. Each path shows its setup steps. Website chat, partner feeds, typing a person in and imports are linked from the page, not rebuilt. Nothing is shown as working that the deployment cannot do: no inbound email domain, no public https address, test phone numbers, no partner access. [api + web]
- **R-LSRC-2** Renaming and the consent text are `settings.routing`, audited. A new consent text is a new `consent_disclosures` row. Earlier inquiries keep citing theirs (R-CONSENT-4; history only, D1). Website chats show the new text (R-INBOX-5). Turning a source off or on is 🔒 and audited, and records when. An off source takes nothing on any path, and what arrives is not kept: the intake token and the lead-email address are unrouted; the Google key is refused (Google does not resend; its leads stay downloadable in Google Ads for 30 days); partner-feed leads are skipped, each with a receipt (`lead_source_skips`, reason `SOURCE_OFF`, no lead content) that the page counts ("N leads skipped while this source was off"), and the feed cursor advances, so turning the source back on does not replay them; website chat stops; tracking numbers fall back to the unknown-caller path, so a call is never dropped. The off confirm says this in words, and the card says "Off since <time>". Sources are never deleted. [api + DB + web]
- **R-LSRC-3** A source's intake URL token and its own signing secret are server-generated, shown once and rotated together (🔒, audited `lead_source.webhook_rotated`). Only sha256(token) is stored. The secret is envelope-encrypted in a system-only table. A source with its own secret accepts only its own X-Realty-Signature. A source without one keeps the deployment secret. Old values stop working at once. Concurrent rotations are serialized by the source's credential lock. [DB + api]
- **R-LSRC-4** The routing preview is read-only. It uses the router's own match, candidate facts and selection (core `previewRouting` over the shared candidate query). It reads the rotation cursor but never locks or advances it. It writes and sends nothing. It names, for a lead arriving now with the given type, ZIP and price band: the rule, the person or pond, each skipped member's reason in words, and whether the 60-second reply would be queued. [core + db + api]
- **R-LSRC-5** Agent forwarding is a lead source, its lead-email inbox (with the chosen portals' sender domains) and a direct routing rule, made through the existing endpoints. No ownership path is added (R-OWN-10). The preview shows whether that rule wins. The steps put the address in the portal first, then automatic forwarding (Gmail filter; Outlook "Redirect to"). [web]
- **R-LSRC-6** Every channel creates or attaches a lead only through `ingestInquiryInTx`: lead email, intake webhook, Google lead form, tracked numbers, partner feeds and web chat. The untracked unknown-caller path keeps `create via inbound`. Gmail sync never creates leads (R-GMAIL-14). [architecture test]
- **R-LSRC-7** A provider delivery whose processing failed, or whose run crashed (older than the stale window), is processed again when the provider retries. A delivery that was processed, unrouted or rejected stays as it is. Every handler behind it is idempotent. [api + DB]
- **R-GADS-1** The Google Ads lead form webhook has one fixed URL. The source's key is the only routing input: server-generated and 160 bits; envelope-encrypted and shown only to Owner/Admin; never logged, audited or returned elsewhere; only its sha256 routes; globally unique; never reissued once retired. `app.resolve_google_lead_form` (a pre-tenant definer) resolves it: the current key of an active source in an active tenant. Every failure to resolve is the same 403 with a tenant-less row. A lookup that fails is a 503 with no row. No limiter refuses a request whose key resolves. D2: Google only. [DB + api]
- **R-GADS-2** A delivery is one inquiry through `ingestInquiryInTx`: externalId `gads:<lead_id>`, so a retry is `duplicate_ignored`; the hook's inquiry type; names, emails, phones and US ZIP from the known column ids; unknown fields ignored; values truncated to the intake's limits; campaignRef `gads:<campaign_id>`; adRef from the creative or ad-group id. Non-string unsafe ids are dropped, never rounded. The source's consent text is cited only when the Owner said it is shown on the Google form; otherwise none (history only, D1). The first response as for any form lead. The answer is 200 `{}`. Once the key resolved, only a source turned off answers 4XX. [api]
- **R-GADS-3** `is_test` creates nothing and records the test time. A lead with no usable lead_id, name, phone or email, or one the intake cannot use, creates nothing. It is counted with its time, nothing of it is stored, and the page says to download it from Google Ads. Until a test arrives, the page says Google's test may not reach us. [api + web]
- **R-GADS-4** Owner/Admin turn a source's form hook on, rotate it and turn it off (🔒, audited), serialized per source. A second turn-on is refused with the current state (`GOOGLE_LEAD_FORM_ALREADY_ON`). The page says when the deployment has no public https address. [api + web]
- **R-TRK-1** A live number with purpose `tracking` may be tied to one lead source. The DB refuses a source on any other purpose. Owner/Admin tie or untie it when acquiring the number or later (`settings.numbers`, audited `number.lead_source_changed`). The source is checked before the purchase, and nothing fails after a purchase: a source turned off meanwhile leaves the number untied, and the response says so. [DB + api]
- **R-TRK-2** An unknown caller or texter to a tracked number of an active source becomes a lead through `ingestInquiryInTx`, inside the inbound transaction: externalId `tracking:<call|sms>:<provider ref>`; the number's source as the original attribution; routing by that source's rules; no 60-second reply (default, Q-LI-1); no consent text cited and no grant from one (the caller saw none; the R-CONSENT-4 first-text grant is unchanged). The intake's lock set is taken with the address lock in one sorted call before matching. The ownership-command setting is cleared afterwards. The call or text is then stored, paused and handed back exactly as today. [api]
- **R-TRK-3** A known person who calls or texts a tracked number is threaded as today. Owned, on offer or closed: nothing else happens (no inquiry, no owner change). In a pond or unassigned (including a person an import left unassigned with no route due, D8(2)): a call, or a text that is not STOP, opt-out language, a wrong-number reply or a reaction, also becomes an inquiry from the number's source through `ingestInquiryInTx` (no first response, no cited text), so the re-inquiry rules decide (R-OWN-20, D8(2)). The inquiry attaches to the contact the call or text was threaded on (conversation affinity among records sharing the phone), never to another record with the same phone, and keeps that contact's pond card summary (a call or text carries no structured facts; b9 review ownership-data/F2, F6). A text with declared MMS text parts (R-TWL-11) is decided by `comms.mms_text`, not at ingestion: once every part is read, an inquiry is made only when the Body and every part read as nothing, none looks like a reaction, no part was unreadable or refused, the source is still on and the person is still in a pond or unassigned; the inquiry is therefore late by the job's delay, and with an unreadable part none is made and the handler's notice says so (b9 review sends/F2). An untied number, an off source or a released number keeps today's path; the source is re-read under the delivery's locks (FOR SHARE), so a source turned off while a delivery waits keeps today's path too (b9 review ownership-data/F5). Nothing is dropped. Recording and the ring target are unchanged (module 48 owns voice.ts). The page says the call rings the on-duty person, who may not be the person the lead is offered to. [api + web]

### Photo and video texts (D5)

- **R-MMS-1** Photos and videos travel only on an agent's own one-to-one text. An outbound `mms` message is initiated by the human who attached the media, and it carries no bulk action, assistant action, playbook step run or marketing piece (CHECK `messages_mms_one_to_one_human`). Its channel never changes to or from `mms`. No automated path has a media input: the assistant and the first response, playbooks and nurture, stage automation, saved-search alerts, STOP/HELP replies, agent alerts, approved drafts, bulk, marketing, and every other path that queues through `queueOutboundInTx`. [DB CHECK + trigger + api]
- **R-MMS-2** Only the message's sender attaches media, and only while the message is queued or scheduled. The media come from that sender's own unexpired uploads for the same person, at positions 0–9: at most 10 items, with media and text together at most 5,000,000 bytes. Attaching an upload deletes it in the same transaction, so each upload is used once. Attached media never change, and every outbound `mms` has at least one item at commit (`app.message_media_outbound_guard`, `messages_mms_has_media`). [DB]
- **R-MMS-3** An upload is bound to its tenant, its uploader and its person. Only its uploader, or the system, sees it, uses it or removes it, and nobody can change it. The only files accepted are JPEG, PNG or GIF of at most 5,000,000 bytes, and MP4 or QuickTime videos of at most 500,000 bytes, whose bytes match their type. Each is encrypted with its own DEK (purpose `mms`), like inbound MMS, and expires 24 h after upload by the database clock. Removing, expiring or erasing an unattached upload shreds its object, and that object only. [DB + api]
- **R-MMS-4** Location and other metadata are removed from every photo and video before it is stored, by a byte-level pass that decodes nothing. Image metadata segments and chunks are dropped wherever they appear, along with anything after the image's end marker; a photo keeps its orientation. Video user-data, metadata and uuid boxes are blanked in place. A file the pass cannot walk is refused, and so is one whose cleaned bytes still carry metadata or name a location. The compose box says that location is removed. [core + api]
- **R-MMS-5** A photo text is a text on the one send path. `dispatchOutbound` checks its opt-outs at send time like any SMS: any of the person's phones, removed ones included, person-level do-not-contact and suppressions (D1). A scheduled one keeps its media and is checked again when due. A blocked or canceled one sends nothing, mints no media URL and keeps its media on the message. One whose media are gone at send time is failed before the provider call (`MEDIA_UNAVAILABLE`, shown as "Not sent"), and its send-time touch stands, as for any provider failure. [api + DB guard]
- **R-MMS-6** The provider fetches each item from a URL minted at dispatch. Its token is MAC-bound to (tenant, message, position) and expires 36,000 s plus 20 min after dispatch. The item is served byte for byte with its stored type (nosniff, attachment, no-store), and only while the message is an outbound `mms` in dispatching, dispatch_unknown or sent. Each item can be fetched at most 12 times an hour, HEAD and GET both counting. Every refusal is the same 404: the same status, code and headers, with the body differing only in its request id. The API never logs the token and never puts it in a job payload, an audit row or an API response. It travels only in the provider request; DEPLOY §5 covers the logs in front of the API. [api]
- **R-MMS-7** Sent media follow the message, as inbound MMS do: they are kept with it, shown to whoever sees it, shredded by erasure and purge, and held from blob deletion by a legal hold. The provider's stored copies are deleted 24 h after dispatch, including those of a submit whose outcome stayed unknown, which are found by the sending number and the time. [api + DB]
- **R-MMS-8** The thread shows outbound media on the message bubble, with the message's status in words: scheduled, sending, not sent and why, or not delivered and why (including the provider's media errors). The compose box states the limits in decimal units and refuses, in words and before uploading, anything that cannot be sent. For a video over 500 KB it says to send a link instead. Attached items never silently vanish: no assistant draft replaces them, and every change of state is announced to screen readers. [web]
- **R-MMS-9** The compose box shows how a text will be billed, as SMS parts of the text exactly as it will be sent:
  - GSM-7 holds 160 in a single part and 153 per part, with extension characters counting 2.
  - Any character outside GSM-7 makes the text UCS-2, counted in UTF-16 units: 70 in a single part and 67 per part.
  - A toll-free sender splits at 152 or 66.
  - No character is split across parts.
  - The box names the characters that make the text cost more, and says how many parts they add. It offers a one-tap fix for typographic quotes, and says "Picture message" instead when media are attached.
  - A one-to-one text over 1,600 UTF-16 units is refused, both in the web and in the API.
  [core + web + api]

**Decisions (D5a).**
- No Owner switch. A photo text is the agent's own one-to-one text, with the same sender and the same opt-out checks, and the database excludes bulk and automated media. If one is ever wanted, it is a tenant_settings boolean read by `app.message_media_outbound_guard` and by the tray.
- Location is removed, not just disclosed (R-MMS-4).
- No other file types. Twilio passes types other than JPEG, PNG and GIF through untouched, under a 500 KB limit, and several carriers do not deliver PDFs over long-code MMS (reported). HEIC, HEIF and WebP photos are converted to JPEG in the browser.

### MLS/IDX
- **R-MLS-1** MLS rows are written only by the system actor from a connected feed; an `mls` row needs its connection, modification time and listing brokerage, and an older modification never overwrites a newer one. [DB, smoke]
- **R-MLS-2** Every client-facing or public rendering of an MLS listing (alert, seller update, portal) passes `applyDisplayRules` for its audience and carries the pack's attribution and disclaimer; `display_allowed = false` is never shown to clients, and nothing is shown to clients until the connection's licence pack is marked counsel-reviewed. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL / MLS.** [core + api]
- **R-MLS-3** If feed data is older than the pack's `max_staleness_hours`, saved-search alerts on MLS rows, price-drop and back-on-market firings on MLS rows, and competing sets pause, and the UI says "MLS data as of …". [core + api]
- **R-MLS-4** MLS listings never get marketing pieces and are never presented as the team's own; MLS photos are referenced by URL unless the pack allows re-hosting. [core + DB trigger]
- **R-MLS-5** An `mls` row that mirrors a team listing is linked through `team_listing_id`; the team row is authoritative and the listing is counted once. [core + db]
- **R-MLS-6** A new listing that matches a saved search has exactly one sender: the Phase 1 saved-search alert path (`matchSavedSearches`, category `search_alert`), which module 24 feeds with MLS rows; no trigger playbook sends "new match". [api]

### Behavior-aware nurture
- **R-NURT-1** A trigger playbook (kind `trigger`) follows R-AST-4; each firing is a `playbook_step_runs` row with no enrollment and a unique `trigger_key`, so one fact fires a step at most once per contact and the contact's one live enrollment is untouched. Firings are woken by module 26's per-tenant reconcile handler from their stored `scheduled_for` and run by `executeTriggerRun(stepRunId)` (job `trigger.step`), which re-checks the fact, the approval, the owner and the pause. [DB, smoke + api]
- **R-NURT-2** Triggers fire only from facts: a price decrease or a return to market on a saved or matched listing (keyed per `listing_price_history` row), a closed deal's home anniversary (yearly, `anniversary:{deal}:{yyyy}`, Feb 29 ⇒ Feb 28), a monthly home value (`avm:{contact_address}:{yyyy-mm}`), and a confirmed deal date coming up (module 31). New matches are R-MLS-6; rate moves are deferred. Opens and other signals never fire a trigger. [core]
- **R-NURT-3** Several firings for one contact inside the playbook's approved digest window become one message listing each item with its evidence; the window is approved playbook content, not a send-time cap, and nothing is dropped or deferred for compliance reasons (D1). [core]
- **R-NURT-4** At send time the step re-checks its fact (still active, still at or below the alerted price, date version unchanged); a stale fact makes the run `skipped_ineligible` with a visible reason. [core + api]
- **R-NURT-5** A paused conversation turns a firing into `converted_to_draft`; the DB guard refuses an autonomous send while paused, and inbound ingestion cancels the contact's scheduled trigger runs. [DB guard + api]
- **R-NURT-6** **Retired: rate-move messages are deferred until a licensed rate series (N9) and counsel review (C7) exist (PHASE2_3_PLAN §3).** When they land: one feed card per agent per move citing the series and date, a draft for a contact only on the agent's tap, category `money` (validatePlaybook: rate move ⇒ money), no APR, payments or loan terms. [core]
- **R-NURT-7** Trigger steps fire only for owned contacts, and an agent-scoped trigger playbook only for contacts its owner currently owns (re-checked at send time). [api + DB]
- **R-NURT-8** Trigger sends never count as touches and never change ownership. [core + views]

### Seller updates
- **R-SELL-1** At most one seller-update draft per active team listing with a seller contact per cadence period (dedupe `seller_update:{listing}:{period_start}`); the next due instant is stored on the listing and woken by module 28's reconcile handler; never autonomous (R-AST-17). An unsent draft never stays "ready" with nobody to send to: when its listing has no seller left it is superseded and its explanation says so (b7 review ownership-data/F1). Only an active member drafts and sends a listing's update: a due listing whose agent is not an active member (a member who left keeps their listings) is held (no draft, the instant is not advanced), its open draft is invalidated, and every active Owner/Admin gets one card naming the listing until it has an active agent again (an Owner/Admin changes its agent, or the agent is reactivated) or leaves the market (`PATCH /listings/:id/agent`, `listing.reassign`, audited `listing.agent_changed`); marketing review and remove-it-yourself cards of such a listing go to the Owners/Admins the same way (b7 review ownership-data/F2). [DB + api]
- **R-SELL-2** Every count and statement in an update is a claim with evidence and says where its data stands: "No showings logged in Realty OS this week" (showings are logged by hand until a showing-service partner exists), feedback verbatim, marketing activity only from published pieces, sent invites ("Sent 41 of 45") and activity agents logged (never from exports), and competing MLS listings with attribution or "Competing listings unavailable: no MLS feed connected". Page-view counts are not shown (deferred, PHASE2_3_PLAN §3); email opens never appear; zero activity is said plainly. [core + DB deferred trigger]
- **R-SELL-3** An approved seller update always carries the approver's own recommendation: one shape, `edited_payload.recommendation`, at least 20 characters (the assistant never prefills it). A body edit is not a recommendation. [DB, smoke]
- **R-SELL-4** Approval fails with `SELLER_UPDATE_NEEDS_RECOMMENDATION` without a recommendation and `SELLER_UPDATE_STALE` when showings, feedback or competing data changed after `assembled_through`; one tap re-assembles. [api]
- **R-SELL-5** Each seller recipient gets an informational message through `dispatchOutbound`; an opted-out seller is shown before the tap. [api]

### Marketing and fair housing
- **R-MKT-1** A piece version is born a draft and is approved only by a person acting as themselves; no piece is approved, published or exported unless the brokerage legal name is rendered, no hard fair-housing term remains, every jurisdiction-pack rule passes, and the guard result names the content hash it was computed over, which must equal the piece's own (database-computed over prose, template, bound fields and ordered assets) hash; a guard result missing any of these is a refusal (R-GATE-12 extended to every piece). [DB, smoke]
- **R-MKT-2** Approved copy is frozen with its guard result and its images; any change is a new version, and the old one stays live until the new one is approved; a piece never changes listing, kind or version number. [DB, smoke]
- **R-MKT-3** Bound facts (price, status, beds, baths, sqft, address, open-house time, photos) render live from the team listing and never need re-approval; model-written copy may not contain price or date numerals. [core + api]
- **R-MKT-4** A brokerage identity or pack change re-renders live pieces; one that no longer passes is unpublished from pages we control within 1 h and needs a new version; its external publications (R-MKT-12) are retracted by the system after the provider confirmed the deletion, where the provider's API supports it (**VERIFY** per provider, incl. Instagram), otherwise a feed card tells the agent to remove the post themselves. [api + job]
- **R-MKT-5** Only the team's own listings (`source = 'team'`) get marketing pieces. [DB, smoke]
- **R-MKT-6** `exported` means handed to the agent; the product never says a post went live where it cannot verify it; Facebook Marketplace is export-only. [web copy lint + api]
- **R-MKT-7** A public listing page is served only through `app.public_listing_page` and returns the approved prose, the bound facts, the identity block and the piece's assets read through `marketing_piece_assets` only, never contact data; a retracted page or an off-market listing returns 410. [DB definer + api]
- **R-MKT-8** An open-house invite is email only, one gated `messages` row per recipient linked by `marketing_piece_id`, dispatched only while that piece is an approved or published open-house invite (DB guard); the send reports "Sent 41 of 45; 4 opted out" and blocked recipients do not raise `failed_send` (the exclusion lives in `deriveExceptions`, module 18a). An invite is a mass send, **never a touch** (R-OWN-14, D8): it records no first contact and counts in no scoreboard, goal or waiting-client check, exactly like a bulk send (b7 review sends/F1). A merged-away recipient is not sent to and is reported on its own ("1 merged into another record", naming the survivor to a caller who sees it; b7 review sends/F5). [DB guard + core + api, smoke]
- **R-MKT-9** When a listing goes sold, withdrawn or expired, unsent invites are canceled, the page says so and exports are flagged "may be out of date"; external publications are retracted where the provider supports it, otherwise a feed card tells the agent to remove the post themselves (as R-MKT-4). [api]
- **R-MKT-10** Marketing numbers come only from first-party facts (form submissions, sent invites) or provider-reported numbers labelled with their source; nothing is estimated and no page-view count is shown. [core + web]
- **R-MKT-11** Review requests go to every client the post-closing playbook covers, never filtered by sentiment, feedback or outcome, and never offer an incentive; they run only once `platform:review_requests` is cleared. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL** (FTC consumer-review rule, platform policies). [core]
- **R-MKT-12** (D2) Posting to Facebook Pages and Instagram business accounts (module 29) and to GBP locations (module 53's branch of the same file) goes only through `publishPost` (`marketing/publish.ts`): a person taps as themselves (TX1 inserts a `marketing_publications` row `publishing`); only the system records the provider's outcome (`published` with the provider's post id, `failed`, `unknown`) and a retraction (after the provider confirmed the deletion); `unknown` is reconciled by lookup, never re-posted blindly (one live row per piece and target); a social post goes only to `facebook_page`/`instagram_business`, a GBP post only to `gbp_location`, only to a `connected`/`degraded` connection and only once `platform:fair_housing` is cleared; otherwise export. The product says "Posted" only for a provider-confirmed row. A piece's `published` status means its public page is live or at least one of its publications is `published` (module 29 sets it after a confirmed publication). A new version never touches the old version's external posts (the UI says the earlier post stays on the Page); retracting one is the system's, after the provider confirmed the deletion, where the API supports it (**VERIFY**), otherwise a feed card tells the agent to remove it themselves. Exactly one retraction of a publication at a time asks the provider: a short system transaction claims it first (`retract_claim_id`), the provider call runs outside any transaction, and the outcome clears the claim; a concurrent request never calls the provider and never raises a "remove it yourself" card for a post the other request took down: the single-post form answers `PUBLICATION_RETRACTING`, take-down-all counts it as `inProgress`, and the reconcile pass leaves the post to the claim holder; the provider call is waited for at most 60 s; a claim whose process died is taken again after 5 minutes, and the reconcile pass finishes it whatever the listing; a confirmed deletion takes the post off any open "remove it yourself" card. Two concurrent taps for one piece and account make one live row and one provider call, and the second is refused `PUBLICATION_ALREADY_LIVE` (TX2 locks the piece before the publication row, the order TX1 takes them, so the two never deadlock). If a timed-out post cannot be found without a post id (**VERIFY**), `unknown` stays unknown and the UI says "Check your Page/Instagram". The Meta connection never requests messaging permissions and receives no webhooks (**VERIFY** permission names). [DB, smoke + api + lint]
- **R-FH-1** A hard fair-housing match blocks approval of a piece (DB CHECK on `guard_result`). [DB, smoke]
- **R-FH-2** Assistant output with a hard or soft fair-housing match is never autonomous: a step downgrades to a draft and the draft is flagged "Check this" (content guard, module 25). [core]
- **R-FH-3** The assistant (text and voice) never answers questions about who lives in an area, its safety or its "kind of people"; it hands back with a neutral note. [core + fixtures]
- **R-FH-4** Audience and targeting rules cannot reference protected classes, protected-proxy attributes, prediction output or (for ads) ZIP/neighborhood geography (type + runtime validator). **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL** per jurisdiction. [core]
- **R-FH-5** The fair-housing lexicon is pack data with hard and soft terms and handles negation; its counsel-review state is the `platform:fair_housing` pack, and the UI states when it has not been reviewed. [core + web]
- **R-GBP-1** A review reply is a `review_reply_draft` that always needs a tap; it never repeats the client's name, the price or deal details (privacy guard), and it is posted only through `postReviewReply`. [core + api]
- **R-GBP-2** GBP posts are `gbp_post` marketing pieces under R-MKT-1/2 and are exported unless a connected, approved integration publishes them. D2: they publish only through `publishPost` to a `gbp_location`, the branch module 53 adds (R-MKT-12). [api]

### Transactions and documents
- **R-TXN-1** A template- or document-proposed date drives nothing until a person confirms it; a non-manual confirmed date carries its confirmer, who acts as themselves; a `manual` confirmed date is entered, and any confirmed date is moved, only by a person (imports use source `import` through their definer). [DB, smoke]
- **R-TXN-2** Only confirmed, uncompleted dates drive reminders, risks and client/lender updates; `due_at` (end of the due day in `tenant_settings.default_timezone`, recorded in `basis`) and `reminder_at` are computed at confirm time by `core.confirmDate` and stored; module 31 is the only writer of deal dates (the Phase 1 deal-screen routes call it too) and its reconcile handler repairs a confirmed date whose `due_at` is missing; settings changes never move stored instants (R-OWN-27); completion is `completed_at` alone and clears the reminder. [core + api + DB]
- **R-TXN-3** Risks are R-ACC-6 `milestone_at_risk` exceptions derived by `deriveExceptions` over stored fields only, keyed `milestone:{deal_date}:{due_version}`; queue semantics are R-ACC-1/3. Deal-level risks (e.g. a financed deal with no lender contact) are not flagged; an unconfirmed date is never a risk. [core + job]
- **R-TXN-4** Client and lender updates are `deal_date_upcoming` steps of the approved team `transaction_updates` trigger playbook (template shipped by module 26; firings created by module 31's reminder reconcile handler from `reminder_at`): informational, confirmed dates only, dates as slots, recipients only the deal roles the step names, and of the client roles only the team's own side (K1, batch 8 review: `deals.side` buy → buyer, co_buyer; sell → seller, co_seller; dual → all four; lease → none, owner default: no automatic milestone updates on lease deals; lender, title and attorney exactly as the step names them), applied when the reminder is planned, re-read live when the update is built (`NOT_OUR_CLIENT`) and on the deal screen; any contract, money or wire wording downgrades to a draft. [core + api]
- **R-TXN-5** Moving a confirmed date bumps `due_version` and drops stored instants not recomputed in the same write, so firings and drafts bound to the old version go stale. [DB, smoke]
- **R-TXN-6** Milestone templates are tenant data per side and jurisdiction, shown as unreviewed until counsel reviewed them; no state's contract defaults are hard-coded. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL.** [core + web]
- **R-DOC-1** A document's visibility is its scope's: a deal document follows the deal; a listing document is for the listing's writers (listing agent, Team Lead, tenant-wide) and the members of a listing-side deal on it, never every member (a listing agreement carries commission terms and seller PII); `shared_to_portal` is an explicit staff action and only for clean deal/listing documents. [DB, smoke]
- **R-DOC-2** Extracted contract dates are proposals with page and character-range evidence; extraction is never autonomous (`contract`); it reads the PDF text layer only: a PDF without one says "No text found in this PDF; enter the dates by hand". [core + DB]
- **R-DOC-3** Every upload starts `pending` and unshared whatever the writer says; only the scan (the system) moves it out of `pending`, once (`infected` is final); a document is unreadable until clean except to its uploader and tenant-wide roles; quarantined objects are never streamed; a document's object is readable to staff only through a clean document they can read. [DB, smoke]
- **R-DOC-4** Documents are retained for the configured period after close, with legal hold; only a human Owner/Admin places or lifts a hold or shortens retention (audited); scope, file, uploader and version never change; a held or retained document cannot be deleted, so erasure and purge refuse (the sweep skips such contacts), and erasure shreds the contact's document objects. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL** (licence-law retention). [DB + retention, smoke]

### Home values
- **R-AVM-1** At most one `ok` estimate per address per month, and only an `ok` estimate carries a value; every display shows the provider, the range, the as-of date and "an estimate, not an appraisal". **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL.** [DB + core]
- **R-AVM-2** A home-value step sends on its own only when `platform:avm` is cleared and the estimate is fresh, `display_allowed`, its range is within `avm_max_range_bps` and its change from the prior estimate is within the jump threshold; otherwise it becomes a draft or `skipped_ineligible` with the reason; it never says "unchanged" when no value was available. [core]
- **R-AVM-3** A valuation is requested only for a contact who can still receive the step (preview) at a current address, at most monthly per address (the `avm:{contact_address}:{yyyy-mm}` firing key) and at most monthly per person and house across a merge (every record of the person, the same normalized address; the prior estimate is read the same way). Only the team's buy-side clients qualify (K2, batch 8 review): buyer or co_buyer on a buy or dual deal closed before the month, never through the deal's primary contact alone, never a lease deal. The house is the purchased property: the deal's listing address when it is known and matches one of the contact's current addresses, otherwise the contact's current `home` address; never an address matching a property the person sold through the team (seller or co_seller on a closed sell or dual deal). Re-checked under the contact lock before the answer is stored and again when the note is built (`ADDRESS_NOT_CURRENT`). [api]

### Owner view, goals, calendar
- **R-OWNER-1** Owner-view figures are computed only in a human Owner/Admin transaction (the assistant and system never read `deal_financials`); every number shows its denominator, and deals left out (no commission, no source) are counted and listed. [api + web]
- **R-OWNER-2** Dollars by source sum commission of deals closed in the period by the deal's immutable `lead_source_id`; cost per closing with zero closings is said in words. [core]
- **R-OWNER-3** Velocity is median stage dwell with sample sizes from `app.stage_dwell_norms()` (no new view). The forecast covers the next 90 days in two units, closings and commission: for each stage, the share of deals that reached `closed` within 90 days of entering it over the trailing 365 days (k of n); expected value = the sum over open deals of their stage's share (times expected commission for the dollar unit); low/high = the same sum with the 80 % Wilson interval of each share; stages with n < 10 are left out and counted ("not enough history"). It returns `{low, high, horizonDays, unit, method, samples: {stage, n, k}[], excluded: {thinStages, noCommission, noSource}}`, with the method in one sentence; no per-deal probability. [core]
- **R-OWNER-4** No owner-view schema has per-deal probabilities or confidence fields (snapshot, R-AST-13 extended). [api]
- **R-OWNER-5** Source costs are read and written by a human Owner/Admin (manual rows, as themselves); the system reads and writes only `ads_sync` rows; a row's source, month and origin never change. [DB, smoke]
- **R-PLAN-1** A weekly plan is internal (`weekly_plan`, autonomy internal by CHECK): 3–5 items chosen by `planWeek(goals, pace, slowDeals, openPromises, apptsWithoutOutcome, tasks)` (pure), ranked by deadline first and then by the gap to the goal, each with evidence; accepting creates tasks; nothing is created without the agent's tap. [core + DB CHECK]
- **R-PLAN-2** Goal pace uses the scoreboard definers (0012). The touches method sentence names what the count holds: calls, texts, emails and website-chat replies the member sent themselves, never imported, bulk or the assistant's (D8(1); batch 7 review ownership-data/F7). [api]
- **R-PLAN-3** Items whose source the agent can no longer see are dropped at render; person-level do-not-contact contacts are never suggested. The do-not-contact is the whole person's (any record of it, along merges: `app.contact_person_ids`), and an item whose contact was merged away is dropped (the person lives on the survivor) (b7 review ownership-data/F5). [api + core]
- **R-CAL-1** Calendar links are visible only to their user and the system; Owners and Admins see nothing of a link. Like every audit row, its audit rows (connected, disconnected, revoked: provider and time, never a token or a calendar id) are theirs to read (owner decision D6, 2026-11-11). [DB, smoke]
- **R-CAL-2** Sync is one-way (app → calendar), fenced by `appointments.sync_version` (an older push never overwrites a newer one; `external_pushed_version` records what the calendar accepted); an edit made in the calendar is detected at the next push (etag mismatch) and the appointment is then detached, never overwritten, and the app says so; tokens refresh lazily inside the push. [api + DB]
- **R-CAL-3** Free/busy is read live per request and never stored. [api]
- **R-CAL-4** Revoking or disconnecting stops sync; in-app appointments are unaffected. [api]

### Gmail inbox sync (D2)
Read-only, per user, Phase 2 (modules 68 and 69). **smoke** in this subsection means `rls_smoke_gmail.sql` (187 checks).
- **R-GMAIL-1** Dark until cleared and switched on: links exist only while `platform:gmail` is cleared (`reviewed_by_counsel_at` and `rules.vendor_ready_at`) and `tenant_settings.mail_sync_enabled` is on; only the Owner turns it on, an Admin may turn it off; turning it off or the pack becoming uncleared hides every copy at once and revokes the links in the next reconcile pass (copy deletion: R-GMAIL-13). [DB, smoke]
- **R-GMAIL-2** Own mailbox only: a person links only their own mailbox (`users/me`) through the OAuth callback (the system actor, after reading the mailbox profile; the address is the profile's, and the callback stores its HMAC under the tenant HMAC key, `mailbox_address_hmac`, never changed); one live link per user; a mailbox is live in at most one link across **all** tenants (no shared mailbox, second teammate or second workspace); Owners, Admins and TLs see no link, token or sync state. [DB, smoke]
- **R-GMAIL-3** Least privilege: scopes exactly `https://www.googleapis.com/auth/gmail.readonly` (**VERIFY** that no narrower scope returns bodies; `gmail.metadata` returns none); no send, modify, labels or settings scope; nothing is ever sent, labeled, archived or deleted in Gmail; replies to clients go through `dispatchOutbound`/`canContact` (platform email) or happen in Gmail itself; a token granting more is refused by the callback and cannot be stored (CHECK). Gmail uses its own OAuth client (at least never `include_granted_scopes`), so a calendar grant never rides along and the exact-scope check holds (**VERIFY** Google's granted-scope semantics). Sending through Gmail: not built. [DB CHECK + api]
- **R-GMAIL-4** Tokens: only the refresh token is stored, envelope-encrypted (tenant field key, AAD = table/column/row id, `apps/api/src/crypto/envelope.ts`), decrypted only by the system actor inside a sync pass (module 68's architecture test forbids selecting `refresh_enc` outside `mailSync/token.ts`, and the API never returns it: the database cannot hide the column from the link's own user, because the system actor uses the same role, S-D2-7); access tokens live in memory only; revoking deletes the refresh token in the same transaction (CHECK) and revokes it at Google (best effort, retried); `purge_after` ≤ `revoked_at` + 30 days. [DB + api]
- **R-GMAIL-5** What is stored: a Gmail message only when one of its From/To/Cc/Bcc addresses (normalized, HMAC) equals a **current** email contact point of a live (not deleted, not merged) contact the mailbox owner owns, collaborates on (live) or shares a deal with, **that the mailbox owner confirmed for sync** (`mail_sync_points`: "Sync Gmail with these clients?" at connect, then a one-tap suggestion per new address; S-D2-1) and that is no live link's mailbox in the tenant (the agent's own or a teammate's Gmail is never a client address); a peer, a TL or the system cannot confirm for the agent; one row per (message, matched contact) with that contact's role only, the **count** of other recipients (never their addresses), the subject, the message's own plain text (quoted history and signature stripped best effort; HTML never stored; ≤ 20,000 characters, truncation flagged) and attachment metadata only (name, MIME type, size; ≤ 20; never fetched); never SPAM, TRASH, DRAFT or CHAT; at most `max_messages_per_thread` copies per thread per link (DB); bodies are plain text at rest like `messages.body` (**VERIFY** under N19 whether the security assessment expects field encryption). [DB guard + api]
- **R-GMAIL-6** Visibility: read exactly like the contact's Phase 1 timeline (`app.contact_detail_visible`) plus the mailbox owner for their own link's rows; never a client principal (Layer 1) and never a support actor (RESTRICTIVE, even under an Owner support grant; **VERIFY** Google API Services User Data Policy); unreadable the moment the link, the member, the switch or the pack stops being live, the thread is excluded, the contact point is removed or the mailbox owner withdraws the address's confirmation (`app.mail_copy_live`, S-D2-2, which answers only a caller these rules admit for that copy, so nobody else learns of a disconnect, an exclusion or a withdrawn confirmation); losing the relation (a reassignment) does not hide copies (E216). Visible to the whole timeline audience by default (owner question Q-GMAIL-5; the connect disclosure says so). [DB, smoke]
- **R-GMAIL-7** Private threads: only the mailbox owner (or the system) excludes a thread ("Remove this thread from Realty OS"); its copies are hidden at once and deleted within one sync interval, and later messages in it are never stored; an exclusion is final while the link lives (no un-exclude). There is no "hidden from the team but kept" mode. [DB, smoke]
