- **R-GMAIL-8** Backfill: history at most `mail_backfill_days` (0–90, default 30, Owner-only; **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL**) and at most `backfill_max_messages`; the origin is computed by the DB (live ⇔ Gmail `internalDate` ≥ `connected_at`); older history is refused; backfill never pauses automation or creates handbacks, and neither does a live message older than 1 hour at fetch (R-GMAIL-10); "import again" re-runs the window. [DB + api]
- **R-GMAIL-9** Opt-outs (D1): every Gmail message module 68 fetches **from** an address that matches a current email point in the tenant (live or backfill) runs the same deterministic classifier as platform inbound email (`classifyInbound`, channel `email`), **before and regardless of storage** (an excluded thread, the per-thread cap, an unrelated contact or an unconfirmed address stores nothing but still records the opt-out, S-D2-3, E229): an unsubscribe or keyword revokes email on that point; a wrong-number reply ("wrong person", checked before opt-out language) revokes email on that point with source `wrong_number`, exactly as platform email does (R-CONSENT-11; 0021's confirmed-wrong-number removal can cite it, R-PAR-12); opt-out language sets the person-level do-not-contact (`needs_review`) exactly as platform email does (R-CHAN-3, D-OPTOUT-LANG); module 68 classifies each message once; the consent event cites the stored copy (`{"kind":"synced_gmail","mail_message_id":…}`) or, when no copy is stored, the message (`{"kind":"synced_gmail","gmail_message_ref":"<sha256 hex of link id + Gmail message id>"}`), and survives the copy's deletion; `consent_events_synced_gmail` keys on either citation, so any event citing synced Gmail is a system email revoke (S-D2-5); synced mail never lifts an opt-out. [api + core + DB]
- **R-GMAIL-10** Fresh live inbound pauses: a live message from the contact whose Gmail `internalDate` is within 1 hour of the fetch runs `ingestSyncedMailInTx(tx, contactId, mailMessageId, classification)` (module 68's one flagged function in `comms/inbound.ts`): the Phase 1 lock, pause of enrollments, cancel of queued assistant messages and scheduled trigger runs, and exactly one handback "replied in Gmail" that cites the `mail_message` by id and time, with no body snippet and no `assistant.enrich_handback` job; it bumps the replying contact's conversation `inbound_seq` (only: never `last_inbound_at`, which would make a free-form send informational under R-CONSENT-5 and step around a marketing-only unsubscribe, D1), so a human message scheduled before the reply is held for a re-tap (E27) and a draft written before it is stale (R-AST-6 `DRAFT_STALE`) (b7 review); it writes no `messages` row and does not classify again (module 68 classified once with `classifyInbound`). The handback dedupe key is `email:{contact}:{rfc_message_id_sha256}` when the Message-ID hash is known (shared with platform email, R-GMAIL-12), else one per Gmail message. An older live message (found by a resync or after an outage) and every backfill message are stored and opt-out-classified (R-GMAIL-9) but neither pause nor hand back. [api]
- **R-GMAIL-11** Idempotent incremental sync: Gmail history from the stored `history_id`; unique (link, Gmail message, contact), so replays are no-ops; the cursor advances only after the page's rows commit; a message deleted, trashed or marked spam in Gmail deletes its copies; an expired history id triggers a bounded resync inside the backfill window, never a full-mailbox read (what it finds pauses only under R-GMAIL-10's 1-hour rule); polling: R-OWN-27; no Pub/Sub push in v1 (**VERIFY** Gmail API). [api]
- **R-GMAIL-12** Dedupe with platform email: `messages.rfc_message_id_sha256` holds the RFC 5322 Message-ID hash of platform email (inbound and outbound, written by module 22 as the system, once: trigger `messages_rfc_guard`); a Gmail copy with the same hash for the same contact (or a contact merged into it) is refused by the DB, and a copy that arrived first is deleted when the platform row lands (module 68 reconcile); the platform row wins. The DB refuses a duplicate only of platform email whose row carries the hash: rows saved before module 22 writes it, rows whose Message-ID module 22 could not learn and imported email (which never has one) are not deduplicated, so a backfill can show such a message twice; hence the rollout precondition in the `platform:gmail` notes (`vendor_ready_at` only after 90 days of hashing, or after a one-time repair). One handback: both paths use the handback dedupe key `email:{contact}:{rfc_message_id_sha256}` when the hash is known (module 22 on platform inbound, module 68 in `ingestSyncedMailInTx`), so whichever lands first hands back and the other does not. [DB + api]
- **R-GMAIL-13** Revocation, copy deletion, merge: a user disconnect, member removal (a suspension only pauses the link: R-INT-5), the switch turned off or the pack uncleared (R-GMAIL-1) and provider revocation (`invalid_grant`) revoke the link (the token: R-GMAIL-4). Its copies are hidden at once (RESTRICTIVE `mail_messages_live`) and deleted by module 68's reconcile handler within one sync interval (the same pass deletes copies whose contact point was removed or whose confirmation was withdrawn, S-D2-2) (`poll_interval_seconds`, 120 s by default); that handler also selects revoked links that still have copies, not only links whose `next_sync_at` is due. The link and its exclusions are purged at `purge_after` (≤ 30 d); database backups expire per DEPLOY backup retention (DEPLOY §8: point-in-time window of at least 35 days; **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL**). Erasure: R-DATA-7. Merge: when two contacts are merged, the system re-points the merged contact's copies to the survivor and to the survivor's email point with the same address HMAC, when the mailbox owner confirmed that point (`mail_messages_update_guard`; only `contact_id` and `contact_point_id` change); a copy that would collide with the survivor's own copy of the same Gmail message, or whose survivor point is unconfirmed, is deleted instead (the survivor's address then appears as a suggestion). Otherwise copies are kept while the link lives (**VERIFY WITH COUNSEL**). [DB + retention, smoke]
- **R-GMAIL-14** Never a channel, never in a model: synced mail never becomes a `messages` row (a fresh reply only moves the conversation's inbound counter, R-GMAIL-10), never dispatches, never counts as a touch, speed-to-lead or ownership event, and is never sent to an AI model or used in assistant drafts (**VERIFY** Google Limited Use for AI); evidence cites it by id and time (`evidence_refs.source_type = 'mail_message'`, never quoted: `evidence_refs_mail_not_quoted`); it is read only through module 68's `GET /contacts/:id/mail-messages` (RLS does the visibility); no SQL names `mail_messages` or `user_mail_links` outside `apps/api/src/mailSync/**` (plus `ingestSyncedMailInTx` in `comms/inbound.ts`) and `mailSync/**` imports no model provider (architecture test); link, disconnect, exclusion, import-again, the Owner switch and window changes are audited without content. Because a reply sent from Gmail never stops a first-contact deadline, the product says so: the connect disclosure, and the mailbox owner's own Gmail entry in the thread while that contact's first-contact deadline is open ("Replies you send from Gmail don't count as first contact. Reply here to stop the clock."; batch 7 review sends/F4). The compose box counts a newer synced Gmail from the contact as "wrote last by email" (it never says they wrote last by text under it; ui/F1), and a merged-away record shows no synced Gmail (its copies move to the current record with the next sync pass; ui/F6). [lint + architecture test + api]

### Coaching
- **R-COACH-1** Only calls with a transcript and a recording basis of `announced_all_parties` or `one_party_policy` are reviewed, and only while the Owner turned call scoring on (which needs `platform:coaching` cleared); imported recordings never are. [api + DB]
- **R-COACH-2** A rubric item is met or missed only with a verified transcript quote; otherwise it is `needs_review` and leaves the denominator; the UI shows "6 of 8 checks", never a bare score. [core + DB deferred trigger]
- **R-COACH-3** A call review covers a call a person handled (never an AI-handled call), for the agent and contact of that call, and is visible only to that agent, Owners/Admins and, only when the Owner turned on `tl_sees_call_scores`, that agent's Team Lead, and only while the caller may play the call's recording. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL** (employee monitoring). Only roles that take call reviews (`coaching.view`: Owner, Admin, Team Lead, agent, ISA; never a TC) have calls reviewed or a daily fix: `core.reviewEligible` answers `role_not_reviewed` for a TC's call, and the scan and the daily-fix reconcile skip TCs, so a tip card never opens a page closed to its holder (batch 7 review ui/F5; core + api, not a DB check). [DB, smoke]
- **R-COACH-4** At most one daily fix per agent per local day: the item missed most often in the last 10 scored calls (minimum 3), never one dismissed in the last 7 days; no data ⇒ no card. [core]
- **R-COACH-5** Call scores never feed routing, ownership, prediction, the scoreboard or any automatic consequence. [lint + architecture test]
- **R-COACH-6** No numeric overall score, grade, leaderboard, voice-tone or sentiment analysis is computed or shown. [snapshot test]
- **R-COACH-7** The weekly improving/slipping view compares the same rubric version, shows n on each side and needs ≥ 5 scored calls per window; a TL sees aggregates only (no quotes, call or contact ids). [core + DB definer]

### Voice answering (Phase 3)
- **R-VOICE-1** The assistant only answers inbound, client-initiated calls, under an approved `voice_answering` version; no AI-originated or AI-handled outbound call can exist. [DB, smoke]
- **R-VOICE-2** For an AI call, processing (`answered_at`) starts only after the self-identifying disclosure and the recording announcement both played, whatever the call's final status, and every tool request comes after both; a failed announcement ends the AI leg to voicemail (`fallback_voicemail`, never answered). **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL.** [DB, smoke]
- **R-VOICE-3** It answers only after the ring-through fails (`voice_answer_after_seconds`) or outside routing hours per `voice_answer_when`; the takeover is the telephony dial timeout decided at call start and stored (`ai_takeover_at`), not a sweeper timer. [core + db]
- **R-VOICE-4** Every tool request is decided by `core.voice.decideToolCall` against the closed set and the approved playbook and logged append-only, with arguments limited to a per-tool allowlist of ids, codes and slot instants (no free text); a denied request has no effect. [core + DB, smoke]
- **R-VOICE-5** Scope is qualify, book, take a message, warm-transfer; price, offers, contracts, commission, legal, lending and neighborhood-demographic questions become a message for the agent. [core + fixtures]
- **R-VOICE-6** Voice answering never changes ownership. A live transfer goes only to the contact's current owner, when the owner is accepting leads and answers within the hold (`voice_transfer_hold_seconds`, a pack value, default 20 s); every other caller (unowned, after hours, owner unavailable) is booked into the owner's calendar or leaves a message, and a critical handback goes to the owner or, for an unowned caller, to whoever the normal intake offer reaches. [core + db]
- **R-VOICE-7** Every AI call ends in exactly one handback with transcript evidence; unverified details are needs-review and never become appointment times; a completed AI call without a handback after 10 min is a P1 alert. [api + job]
- **R-VOICE-8** Asked whether it is a person, it answers truthfully; it never claims to be human or uses a real person's voice. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL** (state AI-disclosure laws; FCC treatment of AI voices). [fixtures + content guard]
- **R-VOICE-9** Voice answering ships dark: an AI-handled call needs the Owner-enabled, attested setting in the active compliance policy and the `platform:voice` pack cleared (counsel and the vendor latency spike); only the Owner turns it on, including by re-activating an older voice-on policy version; turning it off is open to Admins. [DB, smoke]
- **R-VOICE-10** Vendor failure or latency beyond the pack threshold falls back to voicemail; the caller is never left in silence. [api]

### Prediction (Phase 3)
- **R-PRED-1** Only features of an approved, counsel-reviewed platform model enter a prediction; a model's features are a subset of the allowed property and first-party features (`years_in_home`, `equity_band`, `recent_saves`, `stated_timeline_days`, `past_client_years`, `recent_permit`), every reason cites a feature of its own model, and the feature type cannot express protected classes, protected proxies, names, demographics, household composition, geography or life events. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL.** [core types + DB, smoke]
- **R-PRED-2** Public-record data is limited to property facts by construction (no person-level column exists); attaching a record needs an exact address and owner-name match. [DB + core]
- **R-PRED-3** Output is a band with reasons and evidence, shown with the model's platform back-test ("of N people flagged in the back-test, M transacted within 90 days"); an approved model always carries one; no probability or confidence appears in any agent-facing schema. [DB + snapshot test]
- **R-PRED-4** Predictions only create feed cards for people; they never feed messages, routing, ownership, pricing, ads or the portal (architecture test). [lint + test]
- **R-PRED-5** Withdrawing a model hides every score computed with it, and every feed card made from them, at once. [DB, smoke]
- **R-PRED-6** Erasure removes scores and property records; person-level do-not-contact contacts are never listed for outreach. [DB + core]

### Ads (Phase 3)
D2: Google Ads only (`ads_google`); Meta ads are out of scope and `ads_meta` is never offered. Every R-ADS rule applies to Google Ads unchanged.
- **R-ADS-1** The ad cap is an append-only version inserted only by the Owner, as themselves, with an attestation. [DB, smoke]
- **R-ADS-2** Every planned budget change cites the cap version in force and an `ad_reallocation` an Owner or Admin approved, as themselves, that names exactly this (campaign, new daily budget); an approval moves each campaign once; at commit, under a per-tenant lock, the tenant-wide daily total (this run's plan, every other campaign's latest planned or applied budget, paused campaigns at 0) never exceeds that version's daily ceiling. Auto-applying shifts is not built (owner decision D-ADS). [DB, smoke]
- **R-ADS-3** Budget changes go only through `ads/dispatch.ts`: planned ledger row first, provider apply with the event id as idempotency key, then applied/failed rows that must match the planned row's run, campaign and amount; a campaign's budget mirror is written only by the system. [lint + api + DB, smoke]
- **R-ADS-4** Every campaign declares the housing special category and passes the targeting validator; an active campaign runs only an approved or published creative of its own listing; no contact-list audiences, lookalikes or prediction output. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL / platform policy.** [DB + core, smoke]
- **R-ADS-5** Hourly reconciliation pauses every campaign once provider-reported month-to-date spend reaches the monthly cap and alerts the Owner; resuming is a tap; the UI says platforms may overspend a daily budget on a given day. [job + core + web]
- **R-ADS-6** A proposal below the minimum sample (5 agreements per campaign in 90 days) is "hold", with the counts shown. [core]

### Client portal (Phase 3)
- **R-PORTAL-1** A client principal reads zero rows from every table and view outside the portal allowlist and can never write. [DB, smoke]
- **R-PORTAL-2** A client sees only its own active account, its unrevoked and unexpired grants (lapse is immediate) on a deal or listing its contact is a party to (checked when the grant is made and on every read), and the unretracted items of those grants, never another deal, listing, account or tenant. [DB, smoke]
- **R-PORTAL-3** Portal items are published copies from client-visible sources of the grant's own deal or listing (confirmed milestones, clean shared documents, showing dates, feedback and seller updates a person shared, client-visible next steps); feedback and seller updates are never rule-published; an item never changes or moves, and its retraction is final. [DB CHECK + trigger + core + api, smoke]
- **R-PORTAL-4** Staff and client sessions are disjoint: separate cookie (`__Host-psid`), resolver, CSRF secret and transaction helper; a staff cookie on a portal route, or the reverse, is 401. [api + lint]
- **R-PORTAL-5** Clients sign in with a single-use magic link (hashed, 15 min) sent through `dispatchOutbound` (opt-outs apply) to the account's login point, which is one of the client's own current emails or phones (composite FK + trigger); sign-in waits for `platform:portal`; the request never reveals whether an address is on file. [api + DB, smoke]
- **R-PORTAL-6** Revoking a grant, removing the client from the deal or listing, or disabling the account hides everything on the next query; revocation and disabling are final (invite again). [DB, smoke]
- **R-PORTAL-7** Client document downloads go through a definer that re-checks the client policy, the item, the document's scope, `shared_to_portal` and `scan_status`, and a 60 s single-use stream token, and are audited. [api + DB definer]

### Texting reactions (D5b)

- **R-REACT-1** An inbound text is a phone's reaction text only when it arrives as SMS without media and its whole body, normalised (NFC; U+200B, U+200C, U+2060, U+FEFF removed; whitespace collapsed), is exactly one quote form of the template table in `core/compliance/reactions.ts` (iPhone en-US and es-419 tapbacks, iPhone emoji and sticker forms, Google Messages en and es emoji forms; add and remove) with nothing before or after; media reactions are not parsed, and web chat, email, calls, MMS with media, imported history and agent alert phones never carry reactions. [core + api]
- **R-REACT-2** A reaction is linked to the team's text only when, under the classifier's normalisation with double quotes folded, its quote equals the whole body of a non-imported outbound `sms`/`mms` message on a record of the same person (the contact the reaction landed on, or another record merged with it: between a Team Lead's merge and its follow-up the team's text still sits on the merged-away record), sent from the number the reaction came in on to the phone it came from, with status `dispatching`, `sent`, `delivered` or `dispatch_unknown`, dispatched before the reaction and created in the 30 days before it (the newest when several are equal); a cut or altered quote is never linked, and an unlinked text is not a reaction. [core + DB trigger]
- **R-REACT-3** A linked reaction is classified by its own words only (the template words and the emoji, with the tenant's keyword lists), never by its quote; every other inbound text is classified exactly as before D5 (property test). When the whole text would have read as an opt-out or a wrong number, the system audits `consent.reaction_quote_not_read` (reason: that reading), the notification and the thread say so plainly, and the thread offers the person-level do-not-contact in two taps to those who may edit the person. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL** (this narrows R-CONSENT-8, R-CONSENT-11 and R-CHAN-3 for quoted words that are provably the team's own). [core + api + web]
- **R-REACT-4** A reaction is a reply exactly like any inbound text (last-inbound times, the inbound sequence, the pause of every contact on the address, cancelled assistant messages and trigger runs, one handback, the feed card, a notification of the same kind and priority). Only the copy changes: the handback's client claim is a fact ("Sam loved “…”") citing the reaction text verbatim, and no copy says "your text". [api]
- **R-REACT-5** The link and the reaction (add or remove; a tapback name, a sticker or the emoji as received) are one `message_reactions` row, written only by the system inside the inbound transaction. A refused or failed write rolls back to a savepoint, is audited `consent.reaction_link_refused`, and the text is classified as before; a deadlock or serialization failure retries the whole ingestion. The row is never updated, is deleted only with either message, and is readable exactly by those who can read the reaction text; the raw text stays on the message, searchable and one tap away. [DB + api]
- **R-REACT-6** The thread shows each linked reaction as a line naming the reaction and the team's text, and the reaction in force on the reacted-to bubble (the latest add, cleared by a matching removal, replaced by a later add; emoji compared with only U+FE0F removed). An unlinked reaction-shaped text shows as an ordinary text. [web]
- **R-REACT-7** The assistant reads a linked reaction as the client's reaction to the team's stored text, never as the client's words: the prompt prints only the reaction (as untrusted data) and the handle of the team's text (added to the bundle when older than the loaded messages), the reaction's evidence source holds only its own words, and draft categories (handback drafts, the approved reply, run drafts) ignore the quote. [core + api]

### Extensions of Phase 1 families
- **R-AST-19** The new assistant kinds have their autonomy fixed by CHECK: `marketing_draft`, `ad_reallocation` (money), `review_reply_draft` (novel_message) and `contract_extraction` (contract) always need approval; `call_review` and `weekly_plan` are internal. [DB]
- **R-AST-20** No new autonomous category is added; new automated outputs (trigger alerts, milestone updates, home values, review requests) run only as steps of an approved playbook in an existing autonomous category. [core + DB]
- **R-AST-21** A decision on an assistant action (approve, reject) is made by a person, as themselves: the database records the decider's role from memberships at that moment, never lets the system or another person decide in someone's name, never rewrites a decision (reverting to `proposed` clears it) and never executes a requires-approval action that was not approved; an `ad_reallocation` is decided by an Owner or Admin. [DB, smoke]
- **R-ACC-6** `milestone_at_risk` is the fifth exception definition (subject `deal_date`, key `milestone:{deal_date}:{due_version}`, owner the deal owner): a confirmed date with `completed_at` NULL that is overdue (`due_at < now`), or due within 48 h (`due_at - now ≤ 48 h`) with no `responsible` party; the evidence names which predicate holds. [core + job]
- **R-OWN-27** Every Phase 2/3 timer stores its instant when set and carries a fence; each is woken by the owning module's per-tenant reconcile handler (`JobHandler.every`, one line in `worker/handlers/index.ts`) rather than a new `app.due_work` branch: MLS and feed sync from `integration_connections.next_sync_at` (module 24, every 60 s), trigger firings from `scheduled_for` (26, 60 s), home values and anniversaries (26, daily), seller updates from `listings.seller_update_next_due_at` (28, 5 min), milestone reminders from `deal_dates.reminder_at` plus the `due_at` repair (31, 5 min), weekly plans (33, hourly, per user and local Monday), daily fixes (36, hourly, per user and local date), prediction refresh (50, nightly per tenant and date), ad reconcile (52, hourly per campaign and hour), Gmail sync from `user_mail_links.next_sync_at`, plus revoked links that still have copies (68, every `platform:gmail` `poll_interval_seconds`, 120 s by default; D2). The voice takeover is the telephony dial timeout decided at call start; calendar tokens refresh inside the push; `portal_grants.expires_at` is compared with `now()` in the policy. Settings changes move only deadlines set afterwards. [core + db]
- **R-DATA-6** Retention for Phase 2/3 data (all **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL**, configurable within ranges): DM/chat bodies as messages; contactless chat sessions 30 d; MLS rows hidden per licence and purged 180 d after off-market; AVM values 13 months or the licence term; property records per licence (≤ 12 months); predictions latest + 90 d; OAuth tokens 30 d after revoke; marketing pieces life of listing + 3 years; documents 7 years after close (3–10); voice recordings as recordings (730 d); voice tool calls (no PII by construction) and ad ledgers kept; call scores with their recording. **Gap (batch 8 review db-public/F5, not built):** no purge of `home_valuations` exists yet; `avm.refresh` now writes monthly rows, app_user has no DELETE, no definer purges them (0057's prediction purge covers `property_records` and `contact_predictions` only) and erasure is the only path; a deleted address leaves its valuations with `contact_address_id` NULL. A system-only, tenant-filtered retention definer is a later migration. D2: DM bodies n/a (no DMs); synced Gmail copies kept while the link lives and deleted within one sync interval after it is revoked; Gmail refresh tokens deleted at revoke, the revoked link and its exclusions purged 30 d after revoke. [DB + retention]
- **R-DATA-7** Erasure cascades to every new contact-scoped table (portal accounts and grants, property records, predictions, call scores, contact documents, linked chat sessions, D2: synced Gmail copies `mail_messages`), shreds the contact's document objects, keeps HMAC opt-outs and ledgers, and still refuses contacts on a deal or with a document on hold or inside its retention period. [DB, smoke]

### Telephony safeguards (lane telephony-safeguards; fakes only, nothing reaches Twilio)
- **R-TWL-1** Kill switch: `TELEPHONY_LIVE_REQUESTS` (default `false` in every environment) gates every outbound Twilio REST request in the adapter, which refuses it before making it with the provider error `TELEPHONY_LIVE_OFF` (sends, calls, number purchase and release, message lookups, registration reads, media and recording fetches and deletes). dispatch and click-to-call refuse a text or call in TX1, before the daily-cap unit and the touch, as a recorded gate deny `TELEPHONY_LIVE_OFF` ("Live texting and calling are off on this deployment."; b9 review ownership-data/F4: the adapter reports the switch, so a refused send is never a touch); the adapter still refuses anything else that reaches it; number purchase answers with the same sentence; it is never an opt-out, never retried and never reconciled. Inbound webhooks keep working. [api]
- **R-TWL-2** A per-tenant daily cap (`tenant_settings.telephony_daily_cap`, default 200, CHECK) on outbound texts and calls through the real adapter, enforced inside the send-time transaction before the provider call by one atomic claim on `telephony_daily_usage` (tenant-local day; counts only grow). Over the cap: a blocked send with the gate reason `DAILY_SEND_CAP_REACHED` ("Today's sending limit for this team is reached."). The platform's STOP confirmation and HELP reply are exempt. [api + DB]
- **R-TWL-3** The telephony webhook paths are a stable public contract (DEPLOY §5.2): each verifies `X-Twilio-Signature` over `PUBLIC_BASE_URL` + the path (never the Host header) and is idempotent on its MessageSid / CallSid / RecordingSid; a documented path that disappears from the route table fails a test. [api]
- **R-TWL-4** A status callback without `From` is accepted: the tenant is the one the signature guard resolved, else one unrouted `webhook_events` row with a `WEBHOOK_UNROUTED` log and a 200; `MessageSid` stays required. Status map: `canceled` → failed (`PROVIDER_CANCELED`), `scheduled` → ignored, `read` → delivered. [api]
- **R-TWL-5** No code path updates an existing number at Twilio: nothing POSTs to `IncomingPhoneNumbers/{sid}` and nothing writes `SmsUrl`, `VoiceUrl`, `StatusCallback` or an application SID, except number purchase (which R-TWL-1 blocks while off). [api, architecture test]
- **R-TWL-6** Request logs never carry `X-Twilio-Signature` (one exported redaction list, request and response headers). [api]
- **R-TWL-7** `OptOutType=STOP` from Twilio, when our own classification is not stop, opt-out language or wrong number, classifies the message as `stop` (matched `OPTOUTTYPE_STOP`) with the usual STOP effects and no platform reply (the provider sends its own). `OptOutType` START or HELP, and a body of YES, change nothing; YES never lifts an opt-out. [api]
- **R-TWL-8** Error 21610 on an agent alert text records the alert phone's SMS revoke exactly as the agent's own STOP does, marks the delivery suppressed `PROVIDER_OPTED_OUT`, audits it and notifies Admins; the agent's own START from that phone restores alerts. (Contact sends: R-CONSENT-12.) [api]
- **R-TWL-9** Error 30032 (toll-free not verified) or 30034 (unregistered A2P 10DLC sender; both VERIFY), from the submit or a status callback: the text fails with `SENDER_NOT_VERIFIED` ("Not delivered: the team's number isn't verified for texting. The Owner has been told."), never an opt-out, never retried or reconciled; in the same transaction THAT NUMBER is marked not ready to text (`phone_numbers.sender_not_verified_at` / `_detail`; never its registration, which other team numbers share: b9 review sends/F1), so the sender check blocks texts from it while calls keep working and every other number keeps texting; audited; one Owner/Admin notice per number per day naming that number and saying other numbers are not affected; the Numbers page shows "Texting paused for this number". "Check registration status" (a provider read, blocked while R-TWL-1 is off) clears it when the carrier reports the campaign approved; attaching a registration clears it too. A system keyword reply (STOP confirmation, HELP reply) never makes a tracking number the conversation's sticky sender. [api]
- **R-TWL-10** A keyword to a toll-free receiving number: the platform sends no STOP confirmation or HELP reply of its own (`optOutConfirmationSender` 'provider' for that message; the carrier answers). Local numbers are unchanged. VERIFY in the pilot test step. [api]
- **R-TWL-11** Text carried in MMS `text/plain` parts (`message_text_parts`, job `comms.mms_text` enqueued in the inbound message's own transaction): each part is read by SID through the adapter (blocked while R-TWL-1 is off), at most 16 KiB, strict UTF-8 with the BOM stripped (a NUL or invalid UTF-8 is unreadable); in one transaction (address lock, then contact locks) the part is marked read, classified, and every revoking kind the Body did not already produce (stop, opt-out language, wrong number) is applied. A part never lifts an opt-out and never gets its own reply; a STOP found only in a part is confirmed by the platform once per message. A keyword reply (this confirmation, and the Body's STOP confirmation or HELP reply) goes from the texted number when it can text, else from the team main number when that can (it still reaches only the phone that texted the keyword), else it is recorded blocked with its reason (b9 review sends/F3). An unreadable, oversized or refused part is a skipped row plus one notice to the handler. The thread shows read parts as plain text and pending ones as "Part of this text hasn't been read yet."; parts never reach an assistant prompt. [api + DB]

## 10. Phase 2-3 edge cases

E162–E200 belong to FUB parity (Part 3 §14; E194–E200: the People filters). The D2 cases (Gmail inbox sync and publishing) are E201–E229. E230–E233 (the FUB migration owner decisions of 2026-11-11) are in Part 3 §14 too. E283 (an Admin turns call reviews off, owner decision 2026-11-11) is listed with the coaching cases. The D5 cases are E290–E309 (E290–E299 photo and video texts, E300–E309 reactions). E310–E312 (owner decision D8 of 2026-11-12: a person's reply in the website chat is first contact, R-OWN-14) and E341–E343 (owner decision D8 of 2026-11-12: a new inquiry from a person an import left unassigned is routed, R-OWN-20), and E313–E340, E352–E354 and E590–E597 (module 22's lead intake: the Lead sources page, Google Ads lead forms, tracking numbers, portal email forwarding; R-LSRC, R-GADS, R-TRK, R-LEM-9..12) close this table.

| # | Case | Handling | Rules |
|---|---|---|---|
| E71 | Facebook DM from someone unknown | **Retired: owner decision D2 (2026-11-11): Messenger/Instagram DMs are out of scope; nothing is received from Messenger.** Old handling: Intake creates "Unknown (Messenger)" with the connection's lead source, routes it; the 60-s reply goes out in Messenger (step channel `inbound_channel`, part of the approved content) | R-INBOX-1, R-INBOX-4 |
| E72 | Agent replies on Messenger 25 h after the client's last message | **Retired: owner decision D2 (2026-11-11): no Messenger replies and no platform window are built.** Old handling: `PLATFORM_WINDOW_CLOSED` before any provider call; compose offered SMS/email if allowed | R-INBOX-6 |
| E73 | Lead texted STOP last week, now writes on Instagram | **Retired: owner decision D2 (2026-11-11): no Instagram inbound exists.** Old handling: SMS stays revoked; Instagram is a separate channel and the person wrote first, so a person can answer there; automation is paused by the inbound | R-CHAN-4, R-INBOX-2 |
| E74 | "Please stop contacting me" arrives on Messenger | **Retired: owner decision D2 (2026-11-11): no Messenger inbound exists; the same language by email, web chat or synced Gmail is E155/E213.** Old handling: Person-level do-not-contact + every channel on every point revoked (`needs_review`); queued sends canceled; the handback says what happened | R-CHAN-3, R-CHAN-2 |
| E75 | Messenger profile name matches an existing contact | **Retired: owner decision D2 (2026-11-11): no Messenger identities are created.** Old handling: Never merged automatically; suggestion card; merge through R-OWN-24 | R-INBOX-1 |
| E76 | The same person messages two of the team's Facebook Pages; STOP on one | Two identities on one contact; the DB guard refuses DMs to both. D2: no DMs exist; the 0018 guard branch and its smoke check stay until the cleanup | R-INBOX-3, R-CHAN-4 |
| E77 | Chat visitor closes the tab mid-conversation | Session idle 30 min ⇒ ended; chat sends to the closed session are denied `CHANNEL_NOT_CONNECTED`; nothing automated goes to the phone or email the visitor typed; the lead card shows it as "typed in chat, not verified" and the agent may add it and call or text. A reply being written when the chat ends is never moved to another channel silently: the box says the chat ended, that the text is still there and where Send goes now, seen and announced (batch 6 review) | R-INBOX-5 |
| E78 | Zillow changes its lead email template | Unparsed fields stay empty; `needs_review` (`PARTIAL_FORMAT`/`UNKNOWN_FORMAT`) with the email encrypted; nothing routes until an Owner/Admin/Team Lead accepts it (the reviewer may type a missing phone/email). 2026-11-11 lead-email slice: an unknown or partial format is always held, even with a phone found (owner requirement) | R-INBOX-9, R-LEM-5 |
| E79 | Same lead arrives by email parse and partner feed | Dedup by `(source, external_id)` or the 10-minute fingerprint | R-OWN-25, R-INBOX-9 |
| E80 | A DM written before the guard knows the channel | Impossible: the channel CHECK and the guard map changed in one migration; an unknown channel cannot be stored, and the guard raises `CHANNEL_UNKNOWN`. D2: applies to every channel; no DM channel is offered | R-GATE-13 |
| E81 | MLS feed contains the team's own listing | Linked by `team_listing_id`; team row authoritative; status mismatch shown; counted once | R-MLS-5 |
| E82 | MLS listing withdrawn while its alert is scheduled | Send-time recheck ⇒ `skipped_ineligible` | R-NURT-4 |
| E83 | Price drops, then rises inside the digest window | The message reflects the current price; no net drop ⇒ skipped | R-NURT-3, R-NURT-4 |
| E84 | Five saved homes drop price the same day | One digested message listing five homes | R-NURT-3 |
| E85 | MLS feed down for three days | Connection `degraded`; seller updates say "MLS data as of …"; MLS-sourced alerts and firings pause | R-MLS-3 |
| E86 | Listing broker opts a listing out of internet display | Never shown to clients (alerts, updates, portal); pending alerts canceled | R-MLS-2 |
| E87 | A week with zero showings logged | The update says "No showings logged in Realty OS this week"; the agent may skip it | R-SELL-2 |
| E88 | Agent taps Send without a recommendation | `SELLER_UPDATE_NEEDS_RECOMMENDATION`; the DB refuses an approval without `edited_payload.recommendation` | R-SELL-3, R-SELL-4 |

| E90 | Price changes after the website page was approved | The page shows the new price (bound fact); no re-approval; prose unchanged | R-MKT-3 |
| E91 | Listing withdrawn with invites unsent | Invites canceled; page 410; exports flagged | R-MKT-9, R-MKT-7 |
| E92 | Pack requires the brokerage name at least as prominent as the team name and the flyer fails | Cannot approve; the reason is shown | R-MKT-1 |
| E93 | Draft says "perfect for young families" | Hard match ⇒ approval blocked until edited; "family room" is not flagged | R-FH-1, R-FH-5 |

| E99 | AVM has no value for a condo | `skipped_ineligible` with "No estimate available"; never "unchanged" | R-AVM-2 |
| E100 | Rates move 30 bp | **Retired: rate moves are deferred (R-NURT-6).** When they land: one feed card per agent citing the series, a draft per contact only on tap | R-NURT-6 |
| E101 | Home anniversary on Feb 29 | Fires Feb 28 in common years | R-NURT-2 |
| E102 | Agent revokes the calendar app in Google | Link `revoked`; in-app appointments unaffected; reconnect card | R-CAL-4 |
| E103 | Agent edits the synced event in Google | Detected at the next push of that appointment (etag mismatch); detached, never overwritten; "Edited in Google; times may differ" | R-CAL-2 |
| E104 | Imported FUB recording | Never reviewed | R-COACH-1 |
| E105 | A rubric quote fails verification | Item `needs_review`; excluded from the denominator | R-COACH-2 |
| E106 | Agent has two scored calls in 14 days | No daily fix ("Not enough calls yet") | R-COACH-4 |
| E283 | An Admin turns call reviews off (owner decision 2026-11-11: Owner on, Admin off, as Gmail sync, except that the system cannot turn call reviews off and an Admin's off needs no re-auth) | Allowed without re-auth: open tips close at once, audited like the Owner's change. Turning them on, Team Lead visibility and the checklist stay the Owner's: an Admin's request that names any of them (even as stored), alone or together with turning reviews off, is refused whole (`OWNER_REQUIRED`; DB `OWNER_ONLY_SETTING`). The off sticks: each request names only the settings it changes and is decided on the settings row as it is (locked), so an Owner's checklist or Team Lead save from a page loaded earlier leaves reviews off, and turning them back on asks for the Owner's password against the row as it is | R-COACH-1, R-COACH-3 |
| E107 | Closed deal without commission | Excluded from dollars, listed as "missing commission" | R-OWNER-1 |
| E108 | A source with cost and zero closings | "— (0 closings)", never $0 or ∞ | R-OWNER-2 |

| E111 | The plan cites a deal reassigned since | Item dropped at render | R-PLAN-3 |

| E114 | Caller asks what the seller would take | Declined politely; message taken; money handback | R-VOICE-5 |

| E118 | Two calls at once from the same unknown number | Address lock ⇒ one contact | R-OWN-25 |

| E120 | Contact has a self-reported PCS timeline | Not representable in the prediction feature type (compile-time test) | R-PRED-1 |
| E121 | Contact flagged "likely" | Feed card only; no message is sent because of it | R-PRED-4 |
| E122 | Public record owner name differs from the contact | Not attached; needs review | R-PRED-2 |
| E123 | Reallocation proposed while at the cap | Moves money between campaigns only; the tenant-wide total stays ≤ the ceiling in force | R-ADS-2 |
| E124 | Owner lowers the cap below month-to-date spend | Reconcile pauses all campaigns for the month (still a tap to resume); nothing retroactive; plans citing the old version are refused | R-ADS-5, R-ADS-1, R-ADS-2 |
| E125 | Grant revoked while the client is browsing | The next query returns nothing | R-PORTAL-6 |
| E126 | Client edits a URL to another deal's item | 404 (RLS) | R-PORTAL-2 |
| E127 | Spouses share one email | One account per contact; each grant explicit; the login link goes to the account's own point | R-PORTAL-2, R-PORTAL-5 |
| E128 | Agent retracts feedback already published | Item retracted; disappears on the next query; the retraction is final | R-PORTAL-3 |
| E129 | A client context queries any staff table or view | 0 rows from every table outside the allowlist (generated sweep) | R-PORTAL-1 |
| E130 | A file with malware is uploaded | Quarantined; never streamed, extracted or shared | R-DOC-3 |
| E131 | Extraction quotes a closing date that is not in the PDF | Needs review; never a milestone until confirmed | R-DOC-2, R-TXN-1 |
| E132 | A Google review names the client and the price | The reply draft omits both; still a tap | R-GBP-1 |
| E133 | Negative review | Reply drafted on tap only; review requests were never gated by sentiment | R-GBP-1, R-MKT-11 |
| E134 | A Page is disconnected and reconnected in the same tenant | **Retired: owner decision D2 (2026-11-11): no Messenger/Instagram identities exist; a Page is a publishing connection only.** Old handling: Same HMACs: identities, history and opt-outs intact; sends were `CHANNEL_NOT_CONNECTED` meanwhile | R-CHAN-1, R-INBOX-7 |
| E135 | A Page moves from tenant A to tenant B | Live in one tenant at a time; B connects it only through the provider callback (proof of control); A's opt-outs stay in A (R-CONSENT-20); in B the window lets the team reply only to people who write first; the connect dialog says so. D2: a Page is a publishing connection only (R-MKT-12); the window part is gone (R-INBOX-6 retired), the one-tenant rule stands | R-INT-3 |
| E136 | Meta webhook for a Page with no connected or degraded connection | **Retired: owner decision D2 (2026-11-11): the product receives no Meta webhooks.** Old handling: `unrouted`, platform alert, nothing delivered | R-TEN-11 |
| E137 | 5,000 chat messages from one IP in a minute | Rate limits then 429; no contact, offer or notification | R-INBOX-5 |
| E138 | A chat visitor types an email matching an owned contact | Never attached: a new contact with only the chat identity, plus a duplicate suggestion "typed in chat, not verified" to the owner; the visitor sees only this session's replies; merging is a human decision (R-OWN-24). Merged as suggested (the earlier record survives), the chat moves with the person: the survivor answers it while it is open, and the merged-away record shows no chat (batch 6 review) | R-INBOX-1, R-INBOX-5, R-TEN-12 |
| E139 | A person-level do-not-contact contact DMs the Page | **Retired: owner decision D2 (2026-11-11): no DMs are received; a do-not-contact contact who writes by email, web chat or synced Gmail is handled the same way.** Old handling: Inbound stored, pause, handback; every reply blocked `PERSON_DO_NOT_CONTACT`; the thread says so | R-CHAN-2 |
| E140 | A Messenger-only contact merges into an SMS contact; one side is do-not-contact | The survivor keeps the earliest `do_not_contact_at` (DB trigger, along merge chains); identities move; pause and opt-outs win; an address added to the survivor later is blocked too. D2: the same holds for a web-chat-only contact; no Messenger-only contact is created. Its chat sessions move with the identity (batch 6 review), so the survivor's summary says the chat is there and, with the do-not-contact, blocked | R-CHAN-2, R-OWN-24 |
| E141 | The MLS sends a modification older than the stored one | Refused (`MLS_STALE_UPDATE`); the sync skips it | R-MLS-1 |
| E142 | A TC edits a date while the agent drafts a client update citing it | `due_version` bumps; approving the draft returns `DRAFT_STALE` | R-TXN-5, R-TXN-4 |
| E143 | A deal spans a DST change | `due_at` is the stored end of day in the tenant zone, computed at confirm time | R-TXN-2, R-OWN-27 |
| E144 | Counsel withdraws approval of a prediction model | Scores computed with it, and their feed cards, are hidden at once | R-PRED-5 |
| E145 | The allocation job runs twice for one day | `(run, campaign, event_kind)` unique and `(approval, campaign)` unique for plans; the second run is refused | R-ADS-3, R-ADS-2 |
| E146 | The ad platform rejects the housing category or targeting | Campaign `rejected` with the platform's reason; no fallback targeting | R-ADS-4 |
| E147 | A co-buyer is removed from the deal | Their grant stops matching a party at once; their next request sees nothing | R-PORTAL-6 |
| E148 | A staff cookie is sent to `/api/portal`, or the reverse | 401; resolvers and cookies are disjoint | R-PORTAL-4 |
| E149 | A staff session sets the portal account GUC | `portal_account_id()` is NULL for staff; staff RLS only | R-TEN-14 |
| E150 | Voice answering turned off, or its playbook revoked, mid-evening | New AI-handled calls are refused by the DB; calls in progress finish with their handback; an Admin cannot turn it back on, even by re-activating the older policy version | R-VOICE-9, R-VOICE-7 |
| E151 | The assistant books a slot the agent just booked in-app | The booking transaction takes the user's advisory lock and re-reads free slots; the assistant offers the next slot | R-VOICE-4 |
| E152 | An open-house email to 900 contacts, 40 unsubscribed | 860 sent, 40 blocked and counted on the send, not 40 exceptions | R-MKT-8 |
| E153 | A forged "Zillow lead" email from an unaligned domain | `needs_review`; no routing to an autonomous first response; nothing is texted to the number inside it | R-INBOX-9 |
| E154 | A chat visitor types someone else's phone number | Nothing automated is sent to it; the agent sees it marked "typed in chat, not verified" | R-INBOX-5 |
| E155 | A chat visitor writes "stop contacting me" | Person-level do-not-contact on the session's own contact only; a later merge into an existing contact shows the do-not-contact on the merge screen and carries it. Every channel of the person is said blocked before the tap ("Asked not to be contacted at all"), texts and email the agent added included, not only the chat (batch 6 review) | R-CHAN-3, R-CHAN-2 |
| E156 | A confirmed inspection deadline passes uncompleted | `milestone_at_risk` (overdue) for the deal owner, in the TL queue within 60 s | R-ACC-6, R-TXN-3 |
| E157 | A confirmed appraisal date is 36 h away with no responsible party | `milestone_at_risk` (unassigned) | R-ACC-6 |
| E158 | A proposed (unconfirmed) date is tomorrow | Never a risk and never reminded until a person confirms it | R-TXN-2, R-TXN-3 |
| E159 | A wet-signed contract is scanned to an image-only PDF | "No text found in this PDF; enter the dates by hand"; no proposals | R-DOC-2 |
| E160 | A tenant tries to connect a Page it does not manage | The callback's proof fails; nothing is linked; the row stays unconfigured. D2: also for a Facebook Page or Instagram business account connected for publishing | R-INT-3 |
| E161 | An Owner approves a shift; the job replays the approval the next day | The second planned row is refused (approval single-use) | R-ADS-2 |
| E201 | A client's Reply-All reaches both platform inbound and the agent's Gmail | Stored and acted on once when the platform row carries the Message-ID hash: the platform row wins; the Gmail copy is refused, or deleted when the platform row lands after it. One handback: both paths use the dedupe key `email:{contact}:{rfc_message_id_sha256}` when the Message-ID hash is known, so whichever lands first hands back and the other does not. A platform row without the hash is not deduplicated, so a backfill can show that message twice: a row saved before module 22 writes the hash (the `platform:gmail` rollout precondition waits such rows out of the backfill window, or a one-time repair hashes them) and imported email (never hashed) | R-GMAIL-12, R-GMAIL-10 |
| E202 | A client writes from an address not on file | Not stored; the agent may add the address to the contact by hand and confirm it (one-tap suggestion), and later mail syncs (earlier mail only through "import again", inside the window) | R-GMAIL-5 |
| E203 | The agent's personal mail (family, doctor, bank) | Never stored: only addresses the agent confirmed for sync (`mail_sync_points`) are matched, so a peer who adds a stranger's address to a contact and makes the agent a collaborator or deal member, or a TL who adds the agent's own mailbox as a contact point, captures nothing; a live mailbox address of the team is never a client address (`MAIL_ADDRESS_IS_A_MAILBOX`) | R-GMAIL-5 |
| E204 | A client is Cc'd on a thread with a lender who is not a contact | Stored for the client (role `cc`); the lender's address is never stored ("and 1 other") | R-GMAIL-5 |
| E205 | The agent Bcc's a client | Stored with role `bcc`; the other recipients only as a count | R-GMAIL-5 |
| E206 | The agent forwards a client's email to a personal friend | No contact address on the forward ⇒ not stored | R-GMAIL-5 |
| E207 | A client forwards a third party's email | Stored as the client's message; quoted history stripped best effort | R-GMAIL-5 |
| E208 | A contract PDF is attached | Metadata only (name, type, size), never fetched; the agent uploads it to documents if wanted | R-GMAIL-5 |
| E209 | A 500-message thread | At most `max_messages_per_thread` copies; the timeline says older messages stay in Gmail | R-GMAIL-5 |
| E210 | A shared mailbox a teammate already linked | Refused (one live link per mailbox) | R-GMAIL-2 |
| E211 | The same Gmail connected in a second workspace | Refused (one live link per mailbox across all tenants) | R-GMAIL-2 |
| E212 | The agent removes a thread | Hidden at once, deleted within one sync interval, later messages never stored; no un-exclude | R-GMAIL-7 |
| E213 | "Please stop emailing me" sent to the agent's Gmail | Person-level do-not-contact (`needs_review`), the same as platform email | R-GMAIL-9 |
| E214 | An old "unsubscribe" inside the backfill | Revokes email on that point | R-GMAIL-9 |
| E215 | The agent leaves the team | Removed: link revoked, token deleted, copies hidden at once and deleted (owner question Q-GMAIL-1, default delete). Suspended: the link is paused, not revoked; copies hidden until reactivation, then sync resumes | R-GMAIL-13, R-INT-5 |
| E216 | A contact is reassigned A → B | B sees A's earlier synced mail like the timeline; A's new mail with that contact is no longer stored | R-GMAIL-6, R-GMAIL-5 |
| E217 | The agent revokes access in their Google account | `invalid_grant` ⇒ `PROVIDER_REVOKED`, handled as a disconnect | R-GMAIL-13 |
| E218 | The stored history id has expired | Bounded resync inside the backfill window; never a full-mailbox read; a live message it finds that is older than 1 hour is stored and opt-out-classified but neither pauses nor hands back | R-GMAIL-11, R-GMAIL-10 |
| E219 | A message is trashed in Gmail | Its copies are deleted in the next pass | R-GMAIL-11 |
| E220 | A support actor under an Owner grant opens the contact | No synced mail (RESTRICTIVE) | R-GMAIL-6 |
| E221 | The client portal | Synced mail never appears (Layer 1 staff only) | R-GMAIL-6 |
| E222 | The Owner turns Gmail sync off | Every copy hidden at once; links revoked in the next reconcile pass and copies deleted within one sync interval | R-GMAIL-1, R-GMAIL-13 |
| E223 | Two contacts are merged | Copies re-pointed to the survivor and its confirmed email point with the same address HMAC (system only); a copy that would collide with the survivor's own copy of the same Gmail message, or whose survivor point is unconfirmed, is deleted instead | R-GMAIL-13 |
| E224 | A contact is erased | The contact's copies are deleted | R-GMAIL-13 |
| E225 | The token also grants `gmail.modify` (incremental authorization) | Refused by the callback; nothing stored (scopes CHECK) | R-GMAIL-3 |
| E226 | A live reply from the client in Gmail | Automation paused; exactly one handback "replied in Gmail", citing the copy by id and time (no snippet), when the message is at most 1 hour old at fetch | R-GMAIL-10 |
| E227 | A Facebook publish times out | `unknown`, reconciled by lookup, never re-posted; the UI never says "Posted" until the provider confirms | R-MKT-12 |
| E228 | Someone DMs the connected Facebook Page | Nothing is received (no messaging webhook); DMs are out of scope (D2) | R-MKT-12 |
| E229 | A client writes "unsubscribe" in a thread the agent excluded | The opt-out is recorded (consent event citing `gmail_message_ref`); nothing is stored | R-GMAIL-9, R-GMAIL-7 |
| E290 | The client texts STOP, or a do-not-contact is recorded, after a photo text was scheduled | STOP cancels it (cancelQueuedSmsTo covers mms); otherwise the send-time check blocks it. Nothing reaches the provider and no media URL is minted. The bubble keeps its photos and says why | R-MMS-5, R-CONSENT-7 |
| E291 | A send names an upload that is someone else's, another person's or another tenant's, or that is expired, removed or already sent; or two sends race for one upload | 409 `MEDIA_UPLOAD_GONE`, identical in every case, and nothing is created; of two racing sends, one wins. The guard refuses a direct attach | R-MMS-2, R-MMS-3 |
| E292 | A bulk text, approved draft, assistant/playbook/stage-automation step, saved-search alert, STOP/HELP reply, agent alert or marketing send tries to carry media | No input accepts media (the bulk fields give `media_one_to_one_only`). The database refuses an automated `mms` row and any attach to one | R-MMS-1 |
| E293 | A video over 500 KB, media plus text over 5 MB, an 11th item or a text over 1,600 characters; or the carrier refuses the media anyway | Refused in the box, in words (with the file's size, in decimal units), before upload, and again by the API (`MEDIA_TOO_LARGE` or a validation error); for a long video the box says to send a link instead. A carrier refusal (11751, 30019, 12300) ends "Not delivered: …" with the reason in words | R-MMS-3, R-MMS-8, R-MMS-9 |
| E294 | A file claims to be a photo or video but is HTML, SVG, PDF, HEIC, audio, a disguised MP4 brand, or a structure the strip cannot walk | `MEDIA_NOT_ALLOWED`; nothing is stored; the declared type is never trusted | R-MMS-3, R-MMS-4 |
| E295 | A photo or video carries GPS location (in Exif, XMP, a segment between scans, a secondary image, data after the end marker, or a video metadata box) | Stored and sent without it, with the photo keeping its orientation. A file whose metadata or location survives cleaning gets `MEDIA_LOCATION_NOT_REMOVED`, with the link advice | R-MMS-4 |
| E296 | The media URL is used with a malformed, repeated, tampered or other tenant's token, a wrong position, after expiry, after the message failed, was canceled or delivered, after erasure, or past the per-item limit | The same 404 every time, for GET and HEAD (same status, code and headers). The token is in no API log line, audit row or response | R-MMS-6 |
| E297 | The agent loses the lead before their scheduled photo text is due (E15) | Blocked with `SENDER_NOT_AUTHORIZED`. The new owner's suggested draft has the words only and says the photos were not carried over. A photo-only text (no words, or only the brokerage line) creates no draft | R-MMS-1, R-MMS-5 |
| E298 | The submit times out (dispatch_unknown) | The media URLs stay valid while the reconcile decides. If it is unresolved after 10 minutes, the message is failed and its URLs stop answering. Two photo texts to the same number in that window with the same words, or with none, cannot be told apart, and both end "we couldn't confirm it went out"; neither is ever resent. 24 h later, the provider's copies are found by the sending number and the time and deleted, with a P2 alert if none is found | R-MMS-6, R-MMS-7, R-GATE-8 |
| E299 | The person is erased, or an unused upload reaches 24 h | Erasure shreds the person's sent media and staged uploads in its transaction, and the 24 h cleanup deletes the provider's copies. The purge job shreds an unused upload | R-MMS-3, R-MMS-7, R-DATA-4 |
| E300 | Sam's iPhone answers the team's "See you at 3" with `Loved “See you at 3”` | Linked (love, add): ❤️ shows under that bubble, with the line "Sam loved “See you at 3”". It pauses, hands back once, and makes the feed card and the notification ("Sam R. reacted to a text") exactly as a reply | R-REACT-1, R-REACT-2, R-REACT-4, R-REACT-6 |
| E301 | Texts that each quote a text the team sent from that number to that phone: `Liked “Let me know if you don’t want any more texts about this one”`, `Loved “No more texts about Oak St, I promise. Talk soon!”`, `Questioned “Wrong number?”`, `Removed a heart from “Do not text after 9”` | None is an opt-out or a wrong number: no consent event, no person-level do-not-contact, no point marked. Each is audited `consent.reaction_quote_not_read`. The notification adds "Not read as an opt-out or a wrong number: check it". The thread says the quoted words come from a text sent to Sam and offers "They asked not to be contacted…" (two taps). The next text is allowed | R-REACT-3 |
| E302 | The same texts, but no stored text matches: the quoted text went to another phone, to another contact or from another team number, was sent 31 days ago, was imported, blocked or failed, or was never sent | Not a reaction: classified exactly as before (person-level do-not-contact with needs_review, or wrong number), and reviewable with "Not an opt-out" | R-REACT-2, R-REACT-3 |
| E303 | Extra words around a reaction, for example `Loved “See you at 3” please stop texting me`, or any text before the verb | Not a reaction, because the parse is anchored. The whole text is classified as before, so the written opt-out counts | R-REACT-1, R-REACT-3 |
| E304 | Google Messages and Spanish forms: `👍 to “See you at 3”` with U+200B around the emoji and U+200A inside the quotes; the older straight-quote forms with or without hair spaces; `👍 a “…”`; `Se quitó 👍 de "…"` and `Se ha quitado 👍 de "…"`; `Le encantó “…”`; the iPhone form `Reacted ‼️ to “…”` | Each links like E300. The emoji is kept as received, joiners, tags and variation selectors included | R-REACT-1, R-REACT-2 |
| E305 | A removal (`Removed a heart from “…”`, `Removed 👍 from “…”`, `Dejó de encantarle “…”`) or a changed reaction | The chip is cleared, or replaced by the later add. A line says "Sam removed a heart from “…”". It is still a reply ("Sam R. removed a reaction from a text") | R-REACT-6, R-REACT-4 |
| E306 | A media reaction (`Loved an image`, `👍 to a photo`, `Le gustó una imagen`) | Not parsed: no quote proves whose words they are. It is an ordinary text, classified exactly as before | R-REACT-1, R-REACT-2 |
| E307 | The quote is cut (for example ending in "…") or a carrier changed a character (VERIFY 1, 9) | Linked only if it still equals a text sent to that phone, ignoring case, spacing, apostrophes and double quotes. Otherwise it is not a reaction and is classified as before | R-REACT-2 |
| E308 | A reaction-shaped text that arrives through web chat or email, as MMS with media, in imported FUB history, from an agent's alert phone, from an unknown number, or from a phone shared by two contacts that lands on the contact the text was not sent to (two people; records of one person merged together are one contact here, R-REACT-2) | Never a reaction: handled and classified exactly as before | R-REACT-1, R-REACT-2 |
| E309 | The assistant drafts after a reaction | The prompt shows "client (sms reaction to the team's text [Sn])" with only the reaction as data. A claim that the client said the team's words fails verification. A quoted price makes neither the draft nor the approved reply "money" | R-REACT-7 |
| E310 | A website-chat lead's agent answers in the open chat before the first-contact deadline | The reply is the period's first touch, written at dispatch TX1 as a text's is (`first_touch_recorded`, `FIRST_TOUCH_CHAT`): no breach, no `untouched_lead`, speed-to-lead answered with its response time, no auto-release, and the first-contact card ("Goes back to the pond … if not contacted") leaves the feed. The scoreboard counts it once as a touch and its period as answered, live and from the rollup. A chat reply after a re-inquiry that started a new period is that period's first touch, never the earlier miss's late fix (R-ACC-2, E68). Owner decision D8 (2026-11-12) | R-OWN-14, R-ACC-2, R-ACC-4 |
| E311 | The assistant answered in the chat first; a Team Lead, then a collaborator, reply in the chat | The assistant's first response is never a touch (R-AST-15). Who counts is the rule of a text: a collaborator's chat reply is the touch; a teammate who is neither the owner nor a collaborator (a Team Lead) may reply, but that is not a touch and the SLA keeps running. The Team Lead's reply answers the client's message, so the owner's waiting card goes, but the owner's first-contact card still leads with Reply in the chat (never "Can't reach them" for a chat-only lead). If the deadline passes, the item says "No human call, text, email or chat reply from the agent or a collaborator", which stays true | R-OWN-14, R-AST-15, R-ACC-2 |
| E312 | The agent's chat reply is refused: the visitor left (the chat ended), the widget was disconnected, or the person asked not to be contacted | Blocked in TX1 (`CHANNEL_NOT_CONNECTED`, `PERSON_DO_NOT_CONTACT`), with its `gate_decisions` row, and never a touch: the lead is breached and auto-released at its deadlines like any untouched lead. A reply the gate allowed whose delivery then fails stays the touch (its instant is TX1), as a text's does, and raises `failed_send`. The chat has ended only once the visitor has gone 30 minutes without a heartbeat (E77): a reply 29 minutes after their last poll is sent and is the touch, though they may have closed the page and never read it | R-OWN-14, R-INBOX-7, R-CHAN-3, E77 |
| E341 | A person the FUB migration brought in unassigned (owner decision B4: their mapped member was suspended) inquires again: a portal post, a partner feed, a lead email or the generic intake | Owner decision D8 (2026-11-12; batch 6 review ownership-sla/F4). Before it, the inquiry was attached and nothing else happened. Now the inquiry is recorded on them (`reinquiry`; no second contact) and they are routed like a returning lead: `reinquiry` `REINQUIRY_ROUTE`, then the routing rules as the system actor, an offer to an eligible member with its timer, `lead_offer` notification and feed card, or a pond. The suspended member is never the target: the rule considers and skips them (`MEMBERSHIP_INACTIVE`); a rule naming only them sends the person to the pond with `NO_ELIGIBLE_AGENT` and the critical notice to Team Leads and Admins. The accept starts the person's own first-touch period (R-OWN-13). No first response. The import report says when they asked again (the inquiry's time, in the team's time) and where the routing took them, and "Assign these people" no longer selects them (E231). One who asked before the ownership commit was routed only once the owners were in (E343), and is counted and described the same way. B4 is otherwise unchanged: nothing is assigned to a suspended member, the migration continues, the report lists who came in unassigned | R-OWN-20, R-OWN-11, R-IMPORT-5 |
| E342 | An unassigned lead awaiting the router (a route is due: after a decline, an expiry, a reopen), or a lead on offer, inquires again; or two inquiries from the same unassigned person arrive at once | Unchanged: the inquiry is attached only (`attached`) and the router routes the lead once; no re-inquiry event and no second routing. Two simultaneous inquiries of one person serialize on the address lock (same phone or email) or on the contact lock (different ones), which intake takes before reading the state, so the second sees what the first did. When the first routing makes an offer, the second attaches. When it sends the person to a pond (nobody eligible, a pond rule), the second follows the pond's setting like any pond lead: by default a second routing (`REINQUIRY_REROUTE`), and with nobody eligible the pond again, with the critical notice again (review D8-R4; whether a pond entry seconds old should hold a re-inquiry back is an open question for the owner). A merge that lands between intake's match and its lock is followed to the record the person lives on now (review D8-R5) | R-OWN-20, R-OWN-25 |
| E343 | Any other unassigned person with no route due inquires again: a FUB person with no owner, a CSV row, two imported people merged before the owners were imported, a person put back by an ownership-stage undo; or a person the contacts stage brought in who asks again before the ownership commit; or a live lead (on offer, in a pond, awaiting the router) is merged into such a person | Routed like E341, with two exceptions. Before the ownership commit (review D8-R1) the person is in the import's creation state and their Follow Up Boss owner is mapped and approved but not yet imported: the inquiry is attached only (`attached`; no routing, offer or notice), so the approved mapping decides who holds them, and the import replays it once the owners are in (job `imports.replay_inquiries` when the ownership stage commits, is skipped, or a revert ends the wait): the mapped member owns them as planned (the record is imported, nothing to document) and hears the inquiry (`REINQUIRY`, a notice and a card); one whose member cannot hold leads (B4) or who has no owner is routed then, like E341, and the report says when they asked (the inquiry's time) and to whom. A contacts undo keeps a person who asked again since (touched, R-IMPORT-10) and then routes them. A live lead merged into such a person (review D8-R3) gives the survivor a route due now (the follow-up routes it once; the agent the loser was on offer to is told the offer was withdrawn), or, from a pond, that pond | R-OWN-20, R-OWN-24, R-IMPORT-5, R-IMPORT-7, R-IMPORT-10 |
| E344 | A sell-side deal records the buyer (another agent's client) as `buyer`; a buy-side deal records the seller as `seller` | The team's milestone updates go only to the team's side (R-TXN-4, K1): no firing is planned for the other side's client, and the deal screen says "the buyer is not this team's client on a sell deal"; a dual deal sends to both sides | R-TXN-4 |
| E345 | A lease deal with a `buyer`-role contact, and its primary contact (`other`) | No automatic milestone update to anyone (owner default); the deal page says lease deals get no automatic updates | R-TXN-4 |
| E346 | After an update is planned the TC corrects a recipient's role to the other side's client role | Re-read live when the update is built: skipped `NOT_OUR_CLIENT` with its sentence, nothing sent | R-TXN-4 |
| E347 | A past seller whose home is the house they sold; a renter on a closed lease deal; a deal's primary contact with no client role; the other side's seller on a buy deal | No valuation is requested and no home-value note fires (K2) | R-AVM-3 |
| E348 | A past buyer's recorded home matches a house they sold through the team (before or after the note was planned) | Never valued; a note planned before the sale closed is skipped `ADDRESS_NOT_CURRENT` when it is built | R-AVM-3 |
| E349 | A deal recipient is merged after the reminder pass planned their update: before it went out, or after it was sent | Before: the loser's run is skipped (`CONTACT_REMOVED`) and the next pass plans it once for the survivor, who now holds the role; after: nothing is sent again and the deal screen says the update was sent to a record since merged (never "nothing planned") | R-OWN-24, R-TXN-4 |
| E350 | Two records of one person are on the same deal, the survivor as `other` (or `lender`, or the other side's client role) and the loser as the team's client | The merge keeps the stronger role, ranked by the deal's side (team client > team co-client > other side's client > lender/title/attorney > other), so updates continue | R-OWN-24 |
| E351 | Duplicates holding the same home address are merged after this month's home-value note went out | No second vendor request and no second note this month; the next month's prior estimate includes the merged record's history | R-AVM-3 |
| E313 | Google retries a lead (same lead_id) | One contact; the second is duplicate_ignored; both answered 200 {} | R-GADS-2 |
| E314 | Google "Send test data" (is_test) | No contact, inquiry or job; "Test lead received <time>". Until one arrives, the page says Google's test may not reach us | R-GADS-3 |
| E315 | Wrong, retired, missing or non-string Google key | 403 and a tenant-less rejected row, nothing else (Google does not retry 4XX) | R-GADS-1 |
| E316 | Unknown column ids, custom answers or new top-level fields | Known fields mapped; the rest ignored and counted; nothing fails | R-GADS-2 |
| E317 | No usable lead_id, name, phone or email (incl. every phone and email invalid) | Nothing created or stored; counted with its time; 200 {}; "download it from Google Ads" | R-GADS-3 |
| E318 | An id sent as a JSON number above 2^53 | Dropped, never rounded | R-GADS-2 |
| E319 | Source turned off, then on; form hook turned off | Off: 403; on again: the same key works; hook off: the key is retired forever | R-LSRC-2, R-GADS-1, R-GADS-4 |
| E320 | Unknown caller to a tracked number | Contact credited to the source and offered by its rule; no cited text or grant from it; no 60-second reply; the call rings as today | R-TRK-2 |
| E321 | Owned or offered person texts or calls a tracked number | Threaded; no inquiry; owner unchanged | R-TRK-3 |
| E322 | Untied number, off source or released number | Today's unknown-caller path ("Unknown +1…", no source) | R-TRK-3 |
| E323 | Call and text from the same new number at once on a tracked number | One contact, one inquiry (address lock) | R-TRK-2, R-OWN-25 |
| E324 | Tracked call races a signed webhook lead with the same phone and source | No deadlock (one sorted lock call); one contact | R-TRK-2 |
| E325 | Tie a source to a non-tracking number | Refused (DB CHECK; NUMBER_NOT_TRACKING) | R-TRK-1 |
| E326 | Lead email forwarded by hand from a consumer mailbox | Held PERSONAL_MAILBOX; only the forwarded block read; the lead's own address kept; Accept needs no retyping | R-LEM-11 |
| E327 | Allowlisting a consumer mailbox domain, or an older allowlist that has one | Refused; a saved entry is ignored and shown as not allowed | R-LEM-11 |
| E328 | Gmail forwarding confirmation reaches a source address | Held FORWARDING_CONFIRMATION naming who asked; never a lead; a forged copy without the aligned pass gets the ordinary hold | R-LEM-12 |
| E329 | Auto-forwarded portal email | Signature intact ⇒ lead; broken ⇒ DKIM_FAILED; source address only in the envelope recipient ⇒ still routed | R-LEM-12 |
| E330 | leadmetadata tags in the body, or an unknown version | Body tags read with spans; an unknown version is not read | R-LEM-10 |
| E331 | Hostile leadmetadata (markup, controls, bidi, thousands of tags, huge values, escaped or commented tags in the message) | Capped, cleaned, linear; never throws; escaped or commented tags not read | R-LEM-10, R-LEM-8 |
| E332 | A catalog portal without a vendor row sends a lead | Generic when labelled; otherwise held UNKNOWN_FORMAT; nothing routes; the page asks for a sample | R-LEM-9 |
| E333 | Webhook credentials rotated | Old URL and secret refused at once; the deployment secret is refused for a source with its own and kept for one without | R-LSRC-3 |
| E334 | Preview while a round-robin is mid-round | Names who a lead arriving now would be offered to; the cursor is unchanged; with nothing else changed, the next real lead goes to that person | R-LSRC-4 |
| E335 | Agent-forwarding rule sits below a catch-all | Preview names the catch-all; the page offers to move the rule to the top | R-LSRC-5 |
| E336 | Forwarding agent suspended, not accepting or at capacity | Preview names the fallback pond and the reason in words | R-LSRC-4 |
| E337 | Consent text changed on a source with a website chat | New chats show the new text; a visitor holding the old one gets the stale-disclosure answer; earlier inquiries keep citing the old text | R-LSRC-2, R-CONSENT-4, R-INBOX-5 |
| E338 | Two Owners rotate a webhook or Google key at once | Serialized; both 200; only the later key works; each response says when its key was made; never a 500 | R-LSRC-3, R-GADS-4 |
| E339 | Deployment without an inbound domain or a public https address, or with test phone numbers | The page says so per path; nothing is offered that cannot work | R-LSRC-1 |
| E340 | First text "STOP" from a new number to a tracked number | Lead created with no grant; the opt-out recorded exactly as for any unknown texter today; only the confirmation reply | R-TRK-2, R-CONSENT-1 |
| E352 | The source is turned off between the acquire check and the number insert | The number is bought and saved untied; the response says so; nothing fails after a purchase | R-TRK-1 |
| E353 | A reviewer tries to accept a forwarding confirmation (even with a typed phone) | Refused; Discard only | R-LEM-12 |
| E354 | A provider retries a lead email whose first processing failed or crashed | Processed on the retry; one lead; a processed delivery stays a duplicate | R-LSRC-7 |
| E590 | Hand forward whose signature carries the forwarder's email and phone; two such forwards of different leads, both accepted | Neither the forwarder's From address, a member's address or alert phone, nor a team number is kept; two contacts, never one | R-LEM-11 |
| E591 | The Google key lookup fails (pool or statement timeout) | 503, no rejected row; never a 403 | R-GADS-1 |
| E592 | A Google lead with values longer than the intake limits | Truncated to the limits; the lead is created; never a 4XX | R-GADS-2 |
| E593 | Two Owners turn on a source's Google form at once | One 200 with the key; the other 409 GOOGLE_LEAD_FORM_ALREADY_ON with the state | R-GADS-4 |
| E594 | Person in a pond, or left unassigned by an import with no route due, calls or texts a tracked number | One inquiry from the source; routed once by the re-inquiry rules; the call rings as today | R-TRK-3, R-OWN-20 |
| E595 | Pond person texts STOP, opt-out language or a wrong-number reply to a tracked number, in the Body or only in an MMS text part | No inquiry, no routing; the opt-out exactly as today (a text with parts is decided after the parts are read; b9 review sends/F2) | R-TRK-3, R-CONSENT-1, R-TWL-11 |
| E596 | leadmetadata names lead_email twice with different values | Held PARTIAL_FORMAT; nothing routes | R-LEM-10 |
| E597 | A lead email, Google lead, feed lead or webhook post arrives while its source is off | Refused and not kept (a partner-feed lead leaves a skip receipt the page counts; turning the source back on does not replay it); the card says "Off since <time>"; the off confirm said so | R-LSRC-2 |
| E380 | Two sends of one team race for the last unit of today's cap (cap-1 used) | The claim is one `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE … WHERE sends < cap` inside each TX1: the second waits for the first's row lock and re-checks on the new count, so exactly one passes; the other is blocked `DAILY_SEND_CAP_REACHED` with its gate_decisions row. A TX1 that rolls back returns its unit | R-TWL-2 |
| E381 | The cap is reached and a contact texts STOP (the platform is the confirmation sender) | The STOP confirmation / HELP reply is opt-out-exempt and cap-exempt: it goes out and claims nothing | R-TWL-2, R-GATE-10 |
| E382 | A status callback arrives without `From` (Twilio's master account, or a fake) | Accepted: the guard's tenant, else one unrouted row + `WEBHOOK_UNROUTED` and 200 (never 422); a missing `MessageSid` is still 422 | R-TWL-4 |
| E383 | Twilio reports `OptOutType=STOP` for a body our classifier reads as opt-out language ("please stop texting me") or a wrong number | Our classification stands (it already revokes): no second classification, no extra revoke; only a body we read as none/help/start becomes `stop` (`OPTOUTTYPE_STOP`) | R-TWL-7 |
| E384 | `OptOutType=START` with body START, or a body of YES, from an opted-out phone | START is the existing keyword path (it lifts only what R-CONSENT-10 allows); `OptOutType` adds nothing; YES is no keyword and lifts nothing | R-TWL-7 |
| E385 | Several texts from one unverified toll-free number fail with 30032 the same day (from submits and status callbacks) | Each fails `SENDER_NOT_VERIFIED`; the registration is marked not ready once (the first event); one Owner/Admin notice per number per tenant-local day; calls from the number still work | R-TWL-9 |
| E386 | STOP appears only in an MMS text part (the Body is empty or harmless), and the job runs twice | The part revokes like a STOP body (every phone of the contact, R-CONSENT-7), the platform confirms once (dedupe on the message id), the second run finds the part read and does nothing | R-TWL-11 |
| E387 | An MMS text part is larger than 16 KiB, has a NUL, is invalid UTF-8, or the fetch is refused (the kill switch included) | A skipped row (`TOO_LARGE`, `CONTENT_MISMATCH`, `FETCH_FAILED`) and one notice to the conversation's handler: "Part of a text from {name} couldn't be read. Check the conversation before you text them." | R-TWL-11 |
| E388 | An agent's verified alert phone sends an MMS whose text part says STOP | There is no message row for an alert phone: the part revokes the agent's alert texts exactly as the agent keyword path does (Admins told) | R-TWL-11, R-TWL-8 |
| E389 | A text part says START (or YES) after the phone opted out | Ignored: a part only ever adds opt-outs, never lifts one | R-TWL-11 |

---

# Part 3: FUB parity (migration `0021_fub_parity.sql`, renumbered at merge)

Part 3 covers the everyday contact-management jobs agents do in Follow Up Boss that Realty OS lacked. The gap analysis is `docs/FUB_PARITY.md`, the build is `docs/PARITY_PLAN.md`, and the proof is `packages/db/test/rls_smoke_parity.sql`. It merges two independent drafts (`docs/parity/draft-a.md`, simplicity-first; `docs/parity/draft-b.md`, risk-first) after a security critic and a simplicity critic (ARCHITECTURE §20, "FUB parity design review").

Parts 1 and 2 stay in force and nothing here weakens them:
- D1 opt-outs are checked in `canContact` and both DB guards, with no override and no bulk exemption.
- FORCE RLS with `(tenant_id, id)` keys.
- Ownership changes only through `applyOwnershipCommand`.
- Every send goes through `dispatchOutbound`.
- The assistant acts on its own only inside approved playbooks.

## 11. Parity tables

### 11.1 New tables (10)

| Table | Purpose | Key columns | Constraints / triggers | RLS (see §12) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `tags` | Tenant tag catalog | id, name (trimmed, 1–64), **name_key** (generated `lower(normalize(name, NFKC))`, UQ per tenant), color (12 palette tokens or NULL), protected_proxy, created_by_kind (`user/import`), created_by_user_id | ≤ 1,000 per tenant (advisory-locked count); lifting `protected_proxy` is Owner-only | read: members; create: members except TC, as self, or system (import); rename/recolor/protect: Owner/Admin/system; delete: Owner/Admin |
| `contact_tags` | The one source of a person's tags | (tenant_id, contact_id, tag_id) PK, added_by_kind (`user/bulk/import/migrated`), added_by_user_id, bulk_action_id, import_batch_id | kind ⇔ provenance column; ≤ 100 per person; contact and tag cascade | read: **contact detail visible**; write: detail visible + as self + not TC, or system |
